# Agent 3 — Regulation & Deadlines — Findings 2026-08-02

(Method: shared research harness — one agent per regulation workstream, adversarial per-claim verification, completeness gate, DETERMINISTIC table render. 11 regulation workstream(s) targeted, 11 returned signals, 202 verified signals. Confidence capped at Medium per single-agent playbook. Table rows ordered by Printec value (size x win), biddable first.)

## What changed since last run

The single biggest new item is the ECB's letter to significant-institution CEOs (ref SSM-2026-0301, 7 July 2026, signed by Claudia Buch), which lands directly on Printec's SSM-footprint bank base across Austria, Greece, Croatia, Slovenia, Slovakia, Cyprus and Bulgaria. Second, the Western Balkans TIPS Clone platform run by Banca d'Italia went live on 20 July 2026, with Bosnia & Herzegovina's domestic instant scheme launching the same day with only three banks connected — leaving most BiH banks, plus the Albania/Kosovo/North Macedonia/Montenegro cohort named for the November 2026 onboarding window, still to integrate. Third, on the digital euro the ECB has moved from policy to build: rulebook v0.91 with Annex A1 on testing, certification and approval was published 2 July 2026, a new RDG workstream G5 was set up specifically for ATM and payment-terminal implementation specifications, and the 36-PSP pilot across 19 national central banks was announced 14 July 2026 (Greek and Croatian participants now named; Bulgaria is not among the 19). Fourth, the final PSR text is verified verbatim — Article 59 creates a standalone impersonation-fraud prevention obligation, Article 88 imposes accessibility requirements on strong customer authentication, and Articles 7/38 close the ATM fee-display and independent-deployer registration loop.

On verification corrections: the widely circulated digital euro holding-limit figure is not supported — the ECB FAQ describes only hypothetical limits from a technical analysis, so that number should be dropped from customer material. The TIBER-EU adopter count is 21 jurisdictions, not 20; PSD3's transposition clock is 21 months after entry into force, and the same 21-month clock governs PSR application. Kosovo's SEPA date in the baseline was wrong by 21 months — its only firm trigger is the November 2026 TIPS Clone window. Serbia did not complete an ISO 20022 cutover in November 2025; two May 2026 Governor's acts put it into formal MT/MX coexistence instead. The EPC address cut-off is confirmed as 15 November 2026 (03:30 CET for SCT Inst and OCT Inst). Several rows could not be confirmed at all this run — the Cyprus EAA transposition, Cypriot VoP go-live, Austrian and Croatian wallet positions, and the DORA subcontracting RTS (EUR-Lex blocked) — and should not be quoted to customers as fact.

## Top opportunities (ranked)

1. **ECB / SSM significant institutions (AT, GR, HR, SI, SK, CY, BG)** — supervisory-response and ICT/operational-resilience remediation programme off the 7 July 2026 Buch letter (XL, High).
2. **Banca d'Italia TIPS Clone cohort (BA, ME, AL, XK, MK)** — instant-payments connectivity and bank-side integration ahead of the November 2026 onboarding window (XL, High).
3. **Czech, Hungarian and Romanian PSPs** — IPR readiness build for receive-by-09/01/2027 and send/VoP for non-euro Member States (XL, High).
4. **All EU-footprint PSPs — PSR Article 59** — impersonation-fraud prevention and detection platform, a new standalone obligation (XL, High).
5. **ECB digital euro G5 / rulebook v0.91 Annex A1** — ATM and payment-terminal implementation specs, testing, certification and approval services (XL, High).
6. **BiH banks outside the launch three (Intesa, Raiffeisen, Sparkasse hold the rest)** — domestic instant-payment onboarding for the unconnected majority (L, High).
7. **Greek banks and merchants (DIAS/IRIS)** — mandatory IRIS acceptance across physical and online commerce, plus EuroPA cross-border P2P from 30/06/2026 (L, High).
8. **Hungarian PSPs under Act XVIII of 2025 / MNB Reg 19/2025** — mandated rural ATM installation and estate rollout across allocated settlements (XL, Medium).

| PSR Article 59 — impersonation ('spoofing') fraud liability | EU-wide (all 10 footprint EU states) | VERIFIED VERBATIM. Article 59 'Payment service provider's liability for impersonation fraud'. Art 59(-1) creates a NEW standalone obligation: 'Payment service providers shall have adequate prevention and robust technical safeguards in place to prevent cases where fraudsters replicate and misuse the payment service provider's communication channels for misleading payment service users into making fraudulent transactions.' Art 59(1): where a consumer was manipulated by a third party pretending to be their PSP using communication channels attributed to that PSP, the PSP must refund the FULL amount, conditional on the consumer notifying the PSP without undue delay and reporting the fraud to the police. Art 59(2): within 15 BUSINESS DAYS of being notified AND provided with the police report, the PSP must either refund or give a justified refusal (signposting ADR/complaint bodies under Arts 90, 91, 93, 94, 95). Art 59(3): no refund where the consumer acted fraudulently or with gross negligence. Art 59(4): the BURDEN OF PROOF is on the PSP, which must first invite the consumer to explain the events, and the consumer's silence 'shall not in itself' found a gross-negligence finding. The refund window is 15 business days, NOT the 10 business days in the 24/07/2026 baseline — baseline CORRECTED. | [Council of the EU, doc ST-8221-2026-INIT (PSR final compromise text) — Article 59](https://data.consilium.europa.eu/doc/document/ST-8221-2026-INIT/en/pdf) | 17/04/2026 | (a) Affected: every retail bank in AT, BG, HR, CY, CZ, GR, HU, RO, SK, SI — and in practice their non-EU sister banks in RS/BA/MK/AL/XK/ME/UA, because group fraud policy is set centrally. (b) Must buy/change: three distinct spends. Art 59(-1) 'robust technical safeguards' against channel spoofing = brand/channel-impersonation defence, SMS sender-ID and caller-ID protection, authenticated customer communications. The 15-business-day clock plus reversed burden of proof forces an auditable, evidence-grade fraud case file per claim — real-time transaction monitoring with retained session evidence (INETCO), behavioural analytics (FICO), fraud/AML case management (IMTF Siron). Banks that cannot prove gross negligence pay, so the ROI case for detection tooling becomes a hard P&L number, not a compliance line. (c) Budget: applies 21 months after entry into force; commit 6-12 months earlier — on a Q1-2027 OJ, procurement through 2027-H1 2028 for a Q4-2028 go-live. | High — Article 59 is in the final compromise text as drafted | Medium | XL | High | Build a Printec one-pager quantifying refund exposure per 1,000 spoofing claims at 15-business-day settlement; lead with Alpha Bank (existing Siron account) and BCR. The gross-negligence/burden-of-proof assessment is a contested legal interpretation — route any client-specific liability question to Printec Compliance/DPO rather than opining. |
| ECB / digital euro Rulebook Development Group — Workstream G5 (ATM and payment terminal implementation specifications) | EU euro area (AT, GR, CY, HR, SI, SK, BG in footprint) | RDG workstream G5 on implementation specifications for ATM and terminal providers. The ECB's sixth RDG progress report (covering 01/11/2025-30/04/2026) states verbatim that the RDG 'set up a new workstream (G5) on implementation specifications for ATM and terminal providers'. The ECB launched the call for candidates in March 2026; it 'closed on 10 April 2026 and the workstreams are expected to begin their work in May 2026'. Declared scope, quoted from the report: 'i) initiation factors for digital euro payments at ATMs and payment terminals (e.g. NFC, QR-code); ii) assessing nexo as a candidate reference implementation for enabling digital euro payments at ATMs and potentially at payment terminals; iii) assessing the integration and deployment of the offline digital euro functionality into the existing certified POS and ATM infrastructure; iv) assessing security standards in the field of POS and ATM; v) analysing the implementation specifications and standards applicable to softPOS and mPOS payment acceptance devices; and vi) exchanging information on the current ATM and payment terminal ecosystem.' Rulebook sections 8, 9 and annex F1 are 'currently placeholders that will be developed throughout the second half of 2026, to finalise the first complete draft rulebook'. ATM = automated teller machine; POS = point of sale; softPOS = card acceptance software running on an ordinary NFC smartphone or tablet; mPOS = mobile POS; NFC = near-field communication, the contactless radio link between card/phone and terminal; nexo = a European standards body for card-acceptance messaging between merchant systems and acquirers. | [ECB — Update on the work of the digital euro scheme's Rulebook Development Group, sixth progress report (July 2026), section 4.2](https://www.ecb.europa.eu/euro/digital_euro/timeline/profuse/shared/pdf/ecb.derdgpr260706_RDG_Progress_Report_July_2026.en.pdf) | 06/07/2026 | (a) WHO: every euro-area footprint bank and acquirer — Alpha/NBG/Piraeus/Eurobank (GR), Bank of Cyprus/Hellenic (CY), Erste/Zagrebacka/RBA/PBZ (HR), NLB/NKBM (SI), SLSP/VUB/Tatra (SK), Erste/RBI/BAWAG (AT) and the Bulgarian majors following euro entry. (b) WHAT: the ECB has put Printec's exact product surface into a single named workstream — ATM application and multivendor software (x-core), NFC/QR acceptance at the ATM, POS and softPOS fleet upgrades, TMS (terminal management system — the platform that pushes software and configuration to a terminal estate) re-parameterisation on Verifone/Castles, and secure-element work for offline. Scope item (iii) is the commercially decisive one: the ECB is explicitly trying to fit offline digital euro into the EXISTING CERTIFIED estate, which points to firmware/software retrofit and re-certification revenue rather than wholesale hardware replacement. (c) WHEN: G5 output feeds rulebook sections due H2-2026 and 2027 and the H2-2027 pilot, with issuance targeted 2029 — so bank capex cases must be written into the 2027 budget round, i.e. commercial engagement now. | High — the workstream is constituted and its rulebook sections are due within 6-12 months | Medium | XL | High | Track G5 deliverables on the ECB rulebook timeline page; obtain the nexo ATM reference-implementation assessment when published; confirm whether NCR Atleos, Verifone and Glory are represented in G5 (the ECB does not publish workstream membership — ask via the RDG constituency route); brief Printec product management on scope item (iii), the offline-into-existing-certified-estate requirement, as the single highest-value design input. |
| ECB — holding limit, cash funding and defunding (digital euro FAQ) | EU euro area | CORRECTS A FIGURE IN WIDE CIRCULATION. The ECB's digital euro FAQ states that it prepared a technical analysis to estimate the potential effects of 'various hypothetical holding limits, following a request that emerged during legislative negotiations', testing 'the different hypothetical holding limits of up to EUR 3,000 per person that the co-legislators asked to be tested'. This is a MODELLED SCENARIO REQUESTED BY THE CO-LEGISLATORS, not a decided or proposed legal limit — the figure repeated in Greek and other national press as 'the' cap has no legal basis, and the higher EUR 4,000 variant has no basis at all. The FAQ also confirms, in conditional language: users would be able to fund a wallet via a linked bank account or by depositing cash; 'anti-money laundering checks would be carried out by the distributing payment service provider during the funding and defunding process, just as it is the case with cash withdrawals and deposits today'; when paying offline 'only the payer and the payee would know the personal transaction details'; the preparation phase ran from November 2023 to October 2025; the pilot starts H2-2027 for 12 months; and the ECB aims to be ready for potential first issuance during 2029 assuming the Regulation is adopted in the course of 2026. Secondary analyses place the cash funding and defunding duty in Article 13(3)(b) of the Commission proposal, obliging PSPs that already provide cash services to offer digital euro funding and defunding with banknotes and coins — the article number is NOT verified against EUR-Lex. | [ECB — FAQs on the digital euro](https://www.ecb.europa.eu/euro/digital_euro/faqs/html/ecb.faq_digital_euro.en.html) | 02/08/2026 | (a) WHO: every euro-area credit institution that operates ATMs or branch cash services, which in the footprint is effectively all of them. (b) WHAT: cash-to-digital-euro funding and defunding at the ATM is the hard hardware consequence. It requires ATM application changes (x-core, NCR APTRA), a wallet NFC or QR interaction at the machine, and it materially raises the value of DEPOSIT and RECYCLING ATMs over dispense-only ones, because cash-in to a digital euro wallet needs an intelligent-deposit or recycling module. It also changes cash forecasting, which is an APTRA OptiCash story. (c) WHEN: the obligation bites at issuance, targeted 2029, and dispense-only fleets take two to three years to refresh, so recycler and deposit refresh business cases should be raised in 2027. | EU co-legislators — Regulation on the legal tender of euro banknotes and coins, 2023/0208(COD) | EU euro area (AT, GR, CY, HR, SI, SK, BG in footprint) | The legal-tender-of-cash Regulation travels in the SAME trilogue package as the digital euro. Member States that fail their access indicators must add or protect access points: new ATMs and self-service kiosks, recyclers to keep rural machines stocked, and — for the continuity-during-disruption obligation — resilient offline-capable ATMs, device telemetry and monitoring, and cash optimisation (APTRA OptiCash) to prove coverage. | EU co-legislators — Digital Euro Regulation, 2023/0212(COD) / COM(2023) 369 | EU euro area (AT, GR, CY, HR, SI, SK, BG in footprint) | Trilogues opened 13/07/2026. Confirmed sequence: Commission proposal 28/06/2023; ECON committee position adopted 05/11/2025 by 43 votes to 14 with 1 abstention, rapporteur Fernando Navarrete Rojas; Council general approach 19/12/2025; a further ECON position on the package 23/06/2026; European Parliament plenary consent in the July 2026 plenary by 416 votes to 169 with 22 abstentions; first political trilogue 13/07/2026, reported to have set the political/technical split and opened the tier-one issues of holding limits and the compensation model. Political agreement is targeted by end-2026. The EP Legislative Train summarises the Council position as providing for 'ECB-set holding limits within a Council-defined ceiling', free basic services for users with regulated fees for additional services, guaranteed fair access to mobile devices for payment providers, and a digital euro functioning both online and offline. LAW-FIRM ANALYSIS ONLY, NOT CONFIRMED IN ANY OFFICIAL TEXT: that Parliament wants the ceiling set by Commission delegated act while the Council wants a Council implementing decision by reinforced qualified majority; that Parliament imposes a near-total prohibition on legal persons holding digital euro; that the Council would end the transitional merchant-fee model after ten years at the latest while Parliament would let it run indefinitely; that Parliament carves out small and micro-enterprises from mandatory acceptance; and — most importantly — that the Council requires the payer to be able to CHOOSE BETWEEN ONLINE AND OFFLINE wherever acceptance is mandatory. NO holding-limit figure is fixed in any adopted text. | [European Parliament Legislative Train Schedule — Digital euro (file 2023/0212(COD), last updated 20/06/2026), for the ECON vote, rapporteur and Council position; ECB RDG sixth progress report 06/07/2026 for the 19/12/2025 general approach and 23/06/2026 ECON position; press (eunews, Table.Briefings, Freshfields) for the plenary vote and the 13/07/2026 trilogue](https://www.europarl.europa.eu/legislative-train/theme-economic-and-monetary-affairs-econ/file-digital-euro) | 13/07/2026 | (a) WHO: every merchant acquirer and every mid and large merchant in AT, GR, CY, HR, SI, SK and BG. Until that is confirmed in the Council text, the defensible commercial line is narrower: both mandates provide for online and offline versions, and the ECB pilot already tests offline person-to-person, so offline capability is a design assumption rather than a proven procurement trigger. Capped merchant fees compress acquirer margin and push acquirers toward lower-TCO outsourced and managed terminal estates, which is Printec's managed-services and TMS proposition. | Bulgarian National Bank (BNB) / Bulgarian PSPs | Bulgaria | CONTESTED LEGAL BASIS — HANDLE WITH CARE. Bulgaria adopted the euro on 01/01/2026 and instant euro transfers to and from euro-area accounts became available to Bulgarian customers from that date following the BISERA6/RINGS migration. The Director of the BNB Payment Supervision Directorate, Mihail Dimov, is reported as stating that a special provision of the Instant Payments Regulation for countries adopting the euro sets Bulgaria's implementation deadline at ONE YEAR AFTER EURO INTRODUCTION, i.e. 01/01/2027, covering the Regulation's obligations including Verification of Payee. The Association of Banks in Bulgaria repeated the 01/01/2027 date on 25/02/2026, with deputy chair of its Payment Infrastructure Committee Radoslav Daskalov saying Bulgarian banks are '99 percent ready' and that almost all banks have started work on the project. | PSR Article 88 — accessibility requirements for strong customer authentication | EU-wide (AT, BG, HR, CY, CZ, GR, HU, RO, SK, SI) | VERIFIED VERBATIM — AND STRONGER THAN THE RESEARCHER REPORTED. PSR Article 88 'Accessibility requirements regarding strong customer authentication'. Art 88(1): without prejudice to the European Accessibility Act (Directive (EU) 2019/882), PSPs must ensure that ALL customers — expressly including persons with disabilities, older persons, those with low digital skills, and 'those who do not have access to digital channels or payment instruments' — have at their disposal at least one means, adapted to their specific situation, enabling them to perform SCA (Strong Customer Authentication — the two-factor check a bank must apply to electronic payments and account access) FREE OF CHARGE; the PSP must also provide support on request and adequately inform users of the available means. IMPORTANT CAVEAT the researcher omitted: 'This requirement does not extend to providing devices to the payment service user.' CRITICAL PROVISION the researcher omitted entirely — Art 88(2): PSPs 'shall not make the performance of strong customer authentication dependant on the exclusive use of a single means of authentication and shall not make the performance of strong customer authentication depend, explicitly or implicitly, on the possession of a smartphone or other smart device', unless the user has agreed. Separately, EBA must issue guidelines 18 months after entry into force on assessing that the independence of two 'inherence' (biometric) SCA elements is fully preserved. | [Council of the EU, doc ST-8221-2026-INIT (PSR final compromise text) — Article 88](https://data.consilium.europa.eu/doc/document/ST-8221-2026-INIT/en/pdf) | 17/04/2026 | (a) Affected: every PSP in the 10 footprint EU states, disproportionately the high-cash, older-demographic markets — GR, BG, RO, HR, HU — where smartphone-only SCA excludes a large minority. (b) Must buy/change: Art 88(2)'s explicit ban on making SCA depend on possessing a smartphone is the sharper selling point than the 'free of charge' limb, because Art 88(1) expressly does NOT oblige the bank to hand out devices. That combination points away from issuing hardware tokens and TOWARD SCA performed on the bank's own attended and self-service estate — ATMs, self-service kiosks and branch devices acting as an authentication point, which the bank already owns. This stacks directly on the EAA/EN 301 549 ATM accessibility workstream Printec already sells (accessible ATM UI, audio jack, tactile keypad) and on Thales HSM / remote key injection for the cryptographic side. Selling Article 88 and the EAA as ONE accessible-self-service programme is the strongest combined pitch in the footprint. (c) Budget: applies 21 months after entry into force, so commit 6-12 months earlier; but the EAA has applied since 28/06/2025, so accessible-terminal spend is ALREADY live — use Article 88 to enlarge an existing EAA business case now rather than to open a new 2028 one. | High | Medium | L | High | Pair with the accessibility-eaa workstream rows; propose a single 'accessible authentication + accessible terminal' bundle to Bank of Cyprus, Alpha Bank and OTP, leading on the Art 88(2) no-smartphone-dependency rule. |
| ECB / digital euro scheme rulebook v0.91 — Workstream B1 (testing, certification and approval) and Annex A1 | EU euro area (AT, GR, CY, HR, SI, SK, BG in footprint) | Digital euro scheme rulebook v0.91 was published on 02/07/2026 together with Annex A1 'Testing, certification and approval' and a wider annex set that now includes Annex D1 front-end implementation specifications — whose five parts cover the distributing PSP, the acquiring PSP, the end-user domain, COMMON SERVICES and, explicitly, the ACCEPTANCE DEVICE — and Annex D2 back-end implementation specifications (eight parts including NFC services, risk and fraud, dispute management and settlement). Workstream B1, initially established in March 2024, was REVIVED in early 2026; its candidate call closed 10/04/2026 and it is expected to start work in May 2026. | ECB — agreements with European standard setters (ECPC/CPACE, nexo standards, Berlin Group) and the envisaged EPC agreement | EU euro area | On 24/04/2026 the ECB signed agreements with three European standardisation bodies to reuse open technical standards for the digital euro: CPACE, developed by the European Card Payment Cooperation (ECPC), supporting contactless tap-to-pay over NFC between a payment device and a payment terminal; nexo standards, which connect merchants' systems with the back-end systems of PSPs and acquirers and 'can be used, e.g. to support payment acceptance and cash-machine transactions'; and Berlin Group standards for alias-based payments (such as a mobile phone number), balance checks and reconciliation. SEPARATELY, the ECB's RDG sixth progress report of 06/07/2026 states that 'a similar agreement is envisaged with the European Payments Council (EPC)', that the EPC QR-code payment standard EPC024-22 'could be used for online POS, e-commerce, m-commerce and, potentially, ATM transactions using QR codes', and that 'technical investigations are underway to explore the potential reuse of other EPC standards, such as Verification of Payee'. The same report notes 'the euro-area currently lacks universally available open standards, e.g. for support across payment terminals'. Cipollone, verbatim: the open digital euro standards 'will provide a European free alternative to current proprietary standards, make it easier for new European providers to enter the market and give European payment service providers and merchants the certainty they need to invest, innovate and compete across the euro area.' VoP = Verification of Payee, where the payer's bank checks that the payee name matches the IBAN before the transfer is confirmed. | [ECB press release — ECB signs agreements with European standard setters to facilitate digital euro payments (three agreements and the Cipollone quote); ECB RDG sixth progress report 06/07/2026, section 4.1 (EPC, EPC024-22, ATM QR, VoP)](https://www.ecb.europa.eu/press/pr/date/2026/html/ecb.pr260424~202f9d832b.en.html) | 24/04/2026 | (a) WHO: every acquirer and ATM deployer in the euro-area footprint. (b) WHAT: this fixes the technical target and removes a major roadmap risk — CPACE contactless kernels in terminals and ATM readers, nexo acquirer-protocol stacks (nexo is explicitly scoped by the ECB to cash-machine transactions, which is the ATM hook), Berlin Group alias and reconciliation APIs, and EPC024-22 QR at POS and potentially at the ATM. Printec's x-core multivendor ATM software and its Verifone/Castles terminal estate both need nexo/CPACE-capable builds. The ATM QR route matters commercially because it is a cheap retrofit path for older dispensers that cannot take an NFC reader — but note the ECB calls ATM QR only 'potentially' in scope, so do not sell it as decided. (c) WHEN: the ECB ties market benefit to adoption of the Regulation, targeted end-2026, so vendor roadmaps must be agreed during 2027. | High — agreements signed and the standards are named; the EPC agreement is stated as envisaged, not concluded | Medium | L | High | Obtain CPACE and nexo conformance requirements; check x-core and NCR Atleos roadmaps against nexo acceptor-to-acquirer messaging; watch for the ECB-EPC agreement and any EPC024-22 ATM QR profile. | Directorate for Safety and Compliance of Industrial Products, General Secretariat for Industry, Ministry of Development — Greek EAA market-surveillance authority for ATMs and payment terminals | Greece | [STANDING — dated 2022; primary-text correction of the baseline] Re-reading the Official Gazette text of Law 4994/2022 (FEK A' 215/18.11.2022) directly: Art.19(2)(a) designates the ΔΙΕΥΘΥΝΣΗ ΑΣΦΑΛΕΙΑΣ ΚΑΙ ΣΥΜΜΟΡΦΩΣΗΣ ΒΙΟΜΗΧΑΝΙΚΩΝ ΠΡΟΪΟΝΤΩΝ (Directorate for Safety and Compliance of Industrial Products) of the General Secretariat for Industry, Ministry of Development and Investments, as the market-surveillance authority specifically for 'τερματικά πληρωμών και τις αυτόματες ταμειακές μηχανές' (payment terminals and automated teller machines). EETT (the telecoms regulator) is competent ONLY for the electronic-communications terminal equipment of Art.3(1)(c) under Art.19(2)(b), and separately for monitoring services in the electronic-communications sector — so the baseline's 'EETT enforces EUR 10k-100k' is WRONG for ATMs and POS. Art.26 as enacted contains NO monetary fine amounts at all: it provides only proportionality criteria and administrative MEASURES (recommendation for corrective action, temporary detention, prohibition of circulation/making available). Art.27(2) delegates the actual fine schedule to a future PRESIDENTIAL DECREE proposed jointly by the Ministers of Development, Finance, Digital Governance and Infrastructure & Transport; no such decree was found published as of 03/08/2026. Art.27(1) separately delegates the market-surveillance infrastructure and mixed inspection teams to a joint ministerial decision. Art.23 obliges banks and financial institutions to adapt processes and documents to Annex I Sections III, IV and VIII. Art.28(3): self-service terminals lawfully used before 28/06/2025 may run to end of economic life, max 20 years from first use; Art.28(1): services may use pre-existing products until 28/06/2030, and service contracts signed before 28/06/2025 survive unchanged for at most 5 years. | [Εφημερίδα της Κυβερνήσεως (Greek Official Gazette), Νόμος 4994/2022, FEK A' 215/18.11.2022 — full PDF downloaded from et.gr and text-extracted locally (Arts.19, 23, 25, 26, 27, 28)](https://www.et.gr/api/DownloadFeksApi/?fek_pdf=20220100215) | 18/11/2022 | (a) AFFECTED: Alpha Bank, National Bank of Greece, Piraeus, Eurobank, Attica Bank, Optima, plus Euronet and Cardlink/Worldline as terminal deployers — and any VENDOR placing ATMs/POS on the Greek market. | Bulgarian banks and their cash-in-transit / cash-handling service providers | Bulgaria | [STANDING — dated 31/07/2025; in force 01/01/2026] BNB Ordinance No.18 of 31/07/2025 on quality control of euro banknotes and coins in cash circulation (Darjaven vestnik No.68 of 19/08/2025, in force from 01/01/2026) applies the Eurosystem recirculation regime in Bulgaria. Re-read in the original: Art.28(1) banks and service providers may process euro banknotes and coins ONLY on machines that have passed manufacturer tests and are on the list published by the ECB (banknotes) or by the European Commission (coins); Art.28(2) those machines are subject to BNB control tests; Art.28(3) BNB does NOT test ATM terminal devices or self-service machines that do not identify the user; Art.28(4) self-service machines may be loaded only with notes checked for authenticity and fitness under ECB Decision ECB/2010/14. Art.29 documented staff training is mandatory and an untrained employee may not operate the machine. Art.30–31: a BNB recognition control test passes ONLY at 100% correct identification of the counterfeit test set; a fitness test passes only if at most 5% of the unfit notes in the test set are categorised as fit. Art.32: a listed machine that FAILS a BNB control test is withdrawn from operation and placed under observation for one month; the service operator must apply for a re-test and the machine may be used again only after passing it; if the re-test also fails, the procedure is repeated OR the machine is withdrawn from operation. Art.35: banks, service providers, financial institutions and bureaux de change must adopt internal rules. Art.36: reporting to BNB through the BNB Integrated Information System per Annex IV of ECB/2010/14 and Art.12 of Regulation (EU) No 1210/2010; any cash-handling outsourcing agreement must be notified within 10 WORKING DAYS of conclusion, amendment or termination; the anti-theft banknote-staining chemical used must be declared with a sample. Art.37: BNB may inspect on site, documentarily, or as a 'таен клиент' [mystery shopper] without identifying itself at the start of the check. | [BNB Ordinance No.18 of 31/07/2025 (PDF, Bulgarian) — re-downloaded from bnb.bg and text-extracted locally](https://www.bnb.bg/bnbweb/groups/public/documents/bnb_law/au_lf_ordinance_18_new_bg.pdf) | 01/01/2026 | (a) WHO: every Bulgarian bank running recyclers, intelligent-deposit ATMs or back-office sorters, plus every CIT/cash-handling outsourcer they use. (c) WHEN: spend is in-year — BNB control tests run through 2026, so remediation cannot be deferred to a 2027 budget. | High — in force and enforceable now | Medium | L | High | Obtain the BNB Issue Department implementing instructions ('указания') issued under Ordinance No.18 and the control-test schedule/procedure; then audit each Bulgarian client's installed base against the current ECB tested-machines list and flag any device not on it. | PSR Articles 59a and 59b — telco and online-platform secondary duties | EU-wide | VERIFIED VERBATIM. Article 59a 'Cross-sectoral cooperation for the purpose of fraud prevention and detection' imposes DUTIES, not direct monetary liability, on telcos/platforms. Art 59a(-1): where payment fraud originates in fraudulent content published online, PSPs must without undue delay inform hosting providers under Art 16 (or Art 22) of the DSA, Regulation (EU) 2022/2065. Art 59a(1): data may be exchanged between PSPs and hosting providers, and between PSPs and providers of electronic communications services (Art 2(4)(b) of Directive (EU) 2018/1972 — the EECC), where there are objectively justified grounds to suspect fraud. Art 59a(2): ECS providers and providers of VLOPs/VLOSEs (Art 33 DSA) MUST establish dedicated communication channels with PSPs, or participate in a communication system or information-sharing mechanism, in compliance with GDPR and Directive 2002/58/EC. Art 59a(3): those providers must run educational measures and scam alerts. Art 59a(4): the Commission and the European Board of Digital Services shall encourage a voluntary EU code of conduct on payment fraud (Art 45 DSA). Art 59a(5): ECS providers must take appropriate organisational and technical measures to detect and prevent impersonation fraud, 'including by means of manipulation of calling line identification or electronic mail address'. Art 59b: VLOPs/VLOSEs must request an authorisation/registration number from every advertiser of a regulated financial service, make best efforts to verify it against the PSD3 registers using automated tools, and refuse the advertisement where the information is not obtained. | [Council of the EU, doc ST-8221-2026-INIT (PSR final compromise text) — Articles 59a, 59b](https://data.consilium.europa.eu/doc/document/ST-8221-2026-INIT/en/pdf) | 17/04/2026 | (a) Affected: footprint banks sit on the PSP side of a new machine-to-machine fraud-intelligence exchange with telcos and platforms — in practice A1/Telekom Austria, Deutsche Telekom (HR/GR/MK/HU), Vodafone (GR/RO/HU), Telekom Srbija, Kyivstar. (b) Must buy/change: banks need both an outbound and an inbound fraud-signal interface — a fraud data-sharing capability with GDPR-safe minimisation, plus DSA Art 16 notice-and-action tooling. Maps to Printec transaction monitoring (INETCO) and AML/case management (IMTF Siron) as the system of record, plus integration/managed services to build the telco and platform connectors. Also a cross-industry consortium opportunity Printec is unusually placed to broker, sitting inside both bank and retail estates across 17 markets. (c) Budget: same 21-month clock; the shared-channel build is a 2028 dependency that must be scoped in 2027 because it needs counterparties, not just software. | High that the duties land as drafted; Medium that a national shared fraud utility emerges in any single footprint market by 2029 | Medium | L | Medium | The PSP-telco data exchange is personal-data processing under GDPR and ePrivacy (Directive 2002/58/EC is expressly preserved in Art 59a(2) and 59a(5)) — do NOT advise a client on the lawful basis; route to Printec's DPO / Compliance team. |
| PSR Articles 38, 39 — open-banking API performance and the end of the fallback interface | EU-wide | VERIFIED VERBATIM, INCLUDING THE NEGATIVE. The word 'fallback' appears ZERO times in the final PSR text (checked by full-text search) — the PSD2 permanent fallback interface is gone, replaced by a dedicated-interface regime with hard performance rules. Article 38 'Availability and performance requirements for the dedicated interface': Art 38(1) ASPSPs (account servicing PSPs, i.e. the banks) must limit planned unavailability and prevent unplanned unavailability and underperformance; 'Unavailability shall be presumed to have arisen when five consecutive requests for access ... receive server error responses or no response ... within 30 seconds'. Art 38(2): at least ONE MONTH advance notice of planned unavailability, except for emergency changes. Art 38(5): EBA to submit draft RTS to the Commission within NINE MONTHS of entry into force, taking into account the number of customers impacted and the types of functionality affected for AIS/PIS providers. Article 39: a competent authority may on request exempt an ASPSP from having a dedicated interface, with EBA RTS on the exemption criteria due ONE YEAR after entry into force, considering 'the size, annual turnover and payments volume' of the ASPSP. | [Council of the EU, doc ST-8221-2026-INIT (PSR final compromise text) — Articles 38, 39](https://data.consilium.europa.eu/doc/document/ST-8221-2026-INIT/en/pdf) | 17/04/2026 | (a) Affected: every ASPSP in the 10 footprint EU states — including the many small Greek, Cypriot, Croatian, Slovenian and Slovak cooperative/regional banks that met PSD2 with a thin API plus a fallback. (b) Must buy/change: API gateway uplift, synthetic monitoring against the 5-error/30-second presumption, published availability/performance reporting, and 24x7 monitoring with a defined recovery time. (c) Budget: the EBA RTS lands ~9 months after entry into force, so technical detail is knowable ~mid-2028 on a Q1-2027 OJ; banks that wait for the RTS will miss the 21-month date, so scoping money must be committed in 2027. | High | Medium | L | Medium | Track the EBA consultation on the Art 38(5) and Art 39(2) RTS — it will define the exact KPI thresholds Printec's monitoring product must report against. |

EPC Guidance Document EPC153-22 version 2.1, issued 05/10/2025, fixes 15/11/2026 (and as of 03h30 CET for the SCT Inst and OCT Inst schemes) as the END DATE for the unstructured address format across all five EPC payment schemes. The version-history table records that the EARLIER COMMUNICATED DATE OF 22 NOVEMBER 2026 HAS BEEN CHANGED INTO 15 NOVEMBER 2026, 'aligned with the November 2026 Swift Standards MX Release date scheduled in the second full weekend of November 2026'. The hybrid address format has been allowed since 05/10/2025 and carries 'No end date set for the time being'. Section 6.3: from 15/11/2026 only structured and hybrid are allowed and 'The use of an unstructured address will no longer be allowed and will hence lead to rejects'. Critically (section 7.4, amended in v2.1) the trigger is the INTER-PSP SPACE SETTLEMENT DATE of 15/11/2026, so pain.001/pain.008 files submitted days or weeks BEFORE that date carrying a Requested Execution/Collection Date (AT-T013) on or after it must ALREADY be structured or hybrid, and the Originator PSP must reject them if not. Scope therefore covers Customer-to-PSP files, not just inter-bank pacs messages, so corporates are directly in scope. The CPMI/BIS PIE Task Force Task Team 3 follow-up report (February 2026) works to an END-2027 target for market infrastructures to be aligned with the CPMI harmonised ISO 20022 data requirements - the executive summary refers to 'the timely and consistent adoption of the harmonisation requirements ahead of the end-2027 target' and states that 'a growing number of market infrastructures are expected to be fully aligned with the data requirements by end-2027'. It documents a consistent TWO-STEP European pattern: unstructured postal addresses removed or hybrid enabled in the NOVEMBER 2026 release, then base messages upgraded to ISO 20022 MESSAGE RELEASE 2025 in NOVEMBER 2027 to enable full external code sets. That November 2027 second wave is recorded identically for T2/Euro1, Poland SORBNET3, Romania ReGIS, Albania AIPS, Ukraine SEP, TARGET DKK, Norway NBO and UK CHAPS - i.e. a footprint-wide SECOND budget cycle the 24/07/2026 baseline did not capture. On T2/Euro1 specifically the report confirms ISO 20022 was introduced in both systems in March 2023, requirement 11 is 'Scheduled 2026' ('Hybrid postal address format implemented; unstructured format scheduled for removal in November 2026'), and the +13/+14 UTC-offset defect 'will be corrected with the T2 R2026.NOV Message Release, ensuring full HVPS+ alignment'. SEPARATELY, AND VERIFIED BY EXHAUSTIVE FULL-TEXT SEARCH: the report contains NO entry at all for SERBIA, BOSNIA & HERZEGOVINA, NORTH MACEDONIA, MONTENEGRO or KOSOVO - zero occurrences of any of those country names in the entire document. None of these five footprint RTGS systems has been assessed against the CPMI requirements. | Wallet-relying parties (incl. banks) and national relying-party registrars | EU | Commission Implementing Regulation (EU) 2025/848 of 06/05/2025 (OJ 07/05/2025) sets the rules for registration of wallet-relying parties in national registers; Article 11 states 'It shall apply from the 24 December 2026'. It was amended by CIR (EU) 2026/1730 of 15/07/2026 (OJ 22/07/2026, in force the 20th day after = 11/08/2026), which: (i) requires Member States to ensure that providers of wallet-relying party REGISTRATION certificates issue them 'in an automated manner and without undue delay after the registration' (new Art. 8(1)); (ii) adds a Union-harmonised 'general access policy' in the registration certificate telling users the relying party may only request the data specified there for the registered intended use (new Art. 8(2)(c)), with wallets obliged to warn users when a relying party asks for more (Art. 8(2)(d)); (iii) replaces certificate-policy standard ETSI EN 319 411-1 v1.4.1 (2023-10) with ETSI TS 119 411-8 V1.1.1 (2025-10) for ACCESS certificates (Annex IV) and with ETSI TS 119 475 V1.2.1 (2026-03) for REGISTRATION certificates (Annex V); (iv) requires relying parties to publish a URL to their privacy policy for the intended use. | [EUR-Lex, CIR (EU) 2026/1730 amending CIR (EU) 2025/848 (OJ L, 2026/1730, 22.7.2026); CIR (EU) 2025/848 Art. 11](https://eur-lex.europa.eu/legal-content/EN/TXT/HTML/?uri=OJ:L_202601730) | 22/07/2026 | (a) Every footprint bank wanting to read wallet attributes must first be registered in its national wallet-relying-party register and hold both an access certificate and a registration certificate. | Magyar Nemzeti Bank (MNB) + Act XVIII of 2025 (ATMtv.) — all Hungarian payment service providers | Hungary | Hungary's compulsory rural ATM mandate is statutory, not just decree-level. Act XVIII of 2025 on the installation of automated banknote dispensing machines (ATMtv.) requires obliged payment service providers (PSPs) to guarantee ATM cash withdrawal in EVERY settlement above 1,000 inhabitants from 31/12/2025 and in every settlement above 500 inhabitants from 31/12/2026. MNB Decree 19/2025 (VI.26.), adopted under ATMtv. §4(2), allocates the obligation settlement-by-settlement using a weighted formula on 2024 data: cash-service fee revenue 20%, debit cards with cash-withdrawal function issued 30%, consumer payment accounts 10%, non-zero-balance retail HUF sight deposits 15%, balance-sheet total 25% (all five weights re-read in the decree text and CONFIRMED). Annex 1 groups the settlements into 80 numbered settlement packages (e.g. package 80 = Torony et al., assigned to OTP Bank Nyrt.), scheduled per NGM Decree 16/2025 (V.29.). §3(3) sets contracting deadlines of 20/07/2025 (NGM §1(a) settlements) and 28/02/2026 (NGM §1(b) settlements), with notification to the MNB via the ERA system by 31/07/2025 and 16/03/2026 respectively. §4 requires a successor PSP to reimburse a predecessor's documented ATM-installation costs incurred within 3 years; the MNB reviews the allocation first after 3 years. | [Nemzeti Jogszabálytár / net.jogtar.hu — 19/2025. The Council agreed its negotiating position on 19/12/2025 (press release 'Single currency: Council agrees position on the digital euro and on strengthening the role of cash'; Council document ST 16695/2025 of 17/12/2025), including an effective prohibition on refusing cash except in narrowly defined cases, a duty on Member States to MONITOR AND SAFEGUARD access to and acceptance of cash, and resilience measures for continuity of cash payments during major disruptions to electronic systems. The cash-payment CONTINUITY/resilience element is a distinct and under-sold angle: offline resilience of the cash channel during electronic-payment outages. A cash-ACCEPTANCE mandate on merchants also keeps cash in the retail loop, protecting POS-plus-cash hybrid propositions. (c) If political agreement lands end-2026 and the regulation applies after a transition, budget commitment falls in 2027–2028. Hungary's mandatory NIS2 cybersecurity audit deadline closed on 30/06/2026. SZTFH announced on 10/07/2026 that of 2,520 registered audit-obligated organisations, 2,132 (84.6%) met the deadline; 1,511 audit reports had been processed and 170 organisations achieved the top rating 'elhanyagolhato kockazattal megfelelt' [compliant with negligible risk]. | Republic of Austria — NISG 2026 (NIS2 transposition) | Austria | RE-VERIFIED, WITH ONE DATE ADDED. Austria's Netz- und Informationssystemsicherheitsgesetz 2026 (NISG 2026), transposing Directive (EU) 2022/2555 (NIS2), was passed by the Nationalrat on 12/12/2025, cleared the Bundesrat on 18/12/2025 and was promulgated as BGBl. I Nr. 94/2025 — the Austrian Parliament's own record for item XXVIII/I/308 confirms all three and describes the act as enacting the 'Netz- und Informationssystemsicherheitsgesetz 2026 - NISG 2026'. NEWLY ESTABLISHED THIS RUN: the BGBl publication date is 23/12/2025, which is what produces the entry into force of 01/10/2026, and the mandatory self-declaration (Selbsterklaerung) falls due 30/09/2027. Affected essential and important entities must register within three months of entry into force, i.e. BY 31/12/2026. Incident reporting is staged: early warning without delay and at the latest within 24 hours, full notification within 72 hours, final or progress report within one month. Penalties: essential entities up to EUR 10 million or 2% of total worldwide prior-year turnover, whichever is higher; important entities up to EUR 7 million or 1.4%. | PSD3 Article 38 — registration regime for independent ATM deployers (IADs) | EU-wide; sharpest in GR, HR, CY, HU, BG, RO (high IAD and tourist-DCC density) | VERIFIED VERBATIM. PSD3 Article 38 'Services enabling cash withdrawals offered by ATM deployers not servicing payment accounts': 'ATM deployers shall not be subject to authorisation but shall register, before taking up activity, with the competent authority of the Member State where the cash withdrawal services are intended to be provided.' The registration file must include the Article 3(3) documentation set (points (a), (b), (e)-(h), (j)-(l), (n), (p), (q)), a description of audit and organisational arrangements, and — critically — the Art 3(3)(j) security control and mitigation measures 'shall indicate how the person registering will ensure a high level of digital operational resilience in accordance with Chapter II of Regulation (EU) 2022/2554' (DORA), including for outsourced software and ICT systems. Registration may be refused or revoked for non-compliance, with justification. Such persons 'shall be treated as payment institutions'. GRANDFATHERING: deployers already operating under the PSD2 Art 3(o) exclusion by 21 months after entry into force may continue WITHOUT registration until 27 months after entry into force; the national register must be updated one week after that date and the information passed to EBA immediately. | [Council of the EU, doc ST-8222-2026-INIT (PSD3 final compromise text) — Article 38 and transitional provisions](https://data.consilium.europa.eu/doc/document/ST-8222-2026-INIT/en/pdf) | 17/04/2026 | (a) Affected: Euronet and the local independent ATM estates in Greece, Croatia, Hungary, Cyprus, Bulgaria and Romania — operators that have never been supervised entities and will now be treated as payment institutions. | ESAs (EBA/EIOPA/ESMA) — DORA oversight structure and Joint Examination Teams | All 10 EU footprint states | RE-VERIFIED WITH A TERMINOLOGY CORRECTION. Operational EU-level oversight of the 19 designated CTPPs is live, and 2026 is the first complete cycle of oversight activities. CORRECTION: there is no 'Joint Oversight Venture (JOV)' and no 'Joint Oversight Director'. The ESAs' own pages state that 'the ESAs are organised through a single joint-Directorate performing the oversight of CTPPs as "one team"'. Per CTPP, one of the three ESAs is designated LEAD OVERSEER, supported by a JOINT EXAMINATION TEAM (JET) established under DORA Article 40 and led by a designated ESA staff member, the 'LO coordinator'. JETs are composed of staff from the ESAs, from the competent authorities supervising the financial entities using the CTPP, and from the NIS authorities supervising the CTPP itself. Coordination runs through the Oversight Forum, a standing Joint Committee sub-committee. Oversight activity is described as designation, risk assessment, planning, execution of oversight examinations, and issuance and follow-up of recommendations; in 2026 the Lead Overseers, with JET input, run risk assessments feeding individual annual oversight plans per CTPP plus a strategic multi-annual plan. The ESAs have published a Guide on DORA Oversight activities. NOT CONFIRMED on the pages read: the specific power to require financial entities to suspend or terminate use of a non-compliant CTPP (that is a DORA Article 42 power and should be cited to the Regulation, not to these pages), and the consultation on technical standards for JET composition. | [EIOPA 'DORA oversight' page and EBA 'DORA oversight' page (ESAs joint oversight framework, both re-fetched this run); ESAs Guide on DORA Oversight activities](https://www.eiopa.europa.eu/digital-operational-resilience-act-dora/dora-oversight_en) | 2026 | (a) AFFECTED: footprint banks indirectly — the escalation endpoint of CTPP oversight is a direction to the BANK regarding its use of a non-compliant provider, which is an existential concentration risk for any bank whose customer channels run on a single hyperscaler. | PSD3 Article 37 — cash at retail without a purchase | EU-wide; highest strategic impact in RO, BG, GR, HR, HU (high-cash, thin rural ATM coverage) | VERIFIED VERBATIM, ALL THREE MONETARY FIGURES. PSD3 Article 37 'Services where cash is provided in retail stores without a purchase' obliges Member States to EXEMPT from the Directive persons providing cash in retail stores independently of any purchase, on three cumulative conditions: '(a) the service is offered at its premises by a natural or legal person selling goods or services as a regular occupation; (b) the amount of cash provided per withdrawal does not exceed EUR 150, or the equivalent in national currency; (ba) the client's withdrawal is subject to strong customer authentication.' 'Member States may adopt lower limit than the one specified in point (b) ... but not lower than EUR 100.' Art 37(1a): Member States may set a daily withdrawal limit per payment account, set at a level adequate for AML/CTF protection and 'not lower than EUR 200'. Art 37(1b): charges must be disclosed to the user before the cash is provided. Art 37(2): without prejudice to Directive (EU) 2015/849 and other AML law. The recital confirms the EUR 150 cap exists to prevent unfair competition with ATM deployers and to stop shops running out of cash. | [Council of the EU, doc ST-8222-2026-INIT (PSD3 final compromise text) — Article 37 and recital](https://data.consilium.europa.eu/doc/document/ST-8222-2026-INIT/en/pdf) | 17/04/2026 | (a) Affected: grocery and fuel retail chains across the footprint, their acquirers, and — as a competitive threat — bank ATM estates in rural RO, BG, GR, HR and HU. (b) Must buy/change: TWO-SIDED for Printec. Opportunity: the SCA condition in point (ba) means cash-at-retail cannot run on a plain card swipe — it needs an SCA-capable terminal and a controlled cash-out flow, i.e. Verifone/Castles POS terminals plus terminal management (TMS) and an acquirer-side flow build, sold to retailers and acquirers. | Ministry of Digital Transformation (MDT) / banks in North Macedonia | North Macedonia | [LOCAL-LANGUAGE PRIMARY] North Macedonia has transposed eIDAS 2. The consolidated Law on Electronic Documents, Electronic Identification and Trust Services (Sluzhben vesnik na RSM 101/19, 275/19, 3/25 and 135/25) carries an explicit alignment footnote to Regulation (EU) 2024/1183 of 11 April 2024 (CELEX 32024R1183) and creates a 'дигитален паричник за електронски идентитет' [digital wallet for electronic identity]. New Article 20-м mirrors eIDAS Art. 5f: public-sector bodies must enable use of a registered European digital identity wallet, and private relying parties other than micro and small enterprises that require strong user authentication for online identification - the text explicitly lists 'банкарство, финансиски услуги' [banking, financial services] - must, on the user's request, enable use of registered European digital identity wallets. Article 26 of the 135/25 amending law: the law enters into force on the eighth day after publication but APPLIES FROM 1 JUNE 2027 ('ќе започне да се применува од 1 јуни 2027 година'). Trust service providers and eID scheme issuers already on the register must align their operations within 12 months of entry into force; implementing by-laws are due within six months. Article 25 provides the law ceases to be in force upon North Macedonia's accession to the EU. CORRECTED PENALTIES: Article 71-а fines the DIGITAL WALLET ISSUER (издавач на дигитален паричник), not bank relying parties, on a size-graduated scale - EUR 500-1,000 micro traders, EUR 1,000-2,000 small traders, EUR 3,000-6,000 MEDIUM traders, EUR 5,000-10,000 large traders (denar equivalent). | [Sluzhben vesnik na Republika Severna Makedonija - Zakon za elektronski dokumenti, elektronska identifikacija i doverlivi uslugi (redaktiski precisten tekst), MDT portal](https://portal.mdt.gov.mk/post-body-files/zakoni-mdt-file-P8rS.pdf) | 2025 | (a) Komercijalna Banka AD Skopje, Stopanska Banka AD Skopje (NBG group), NLB Banka Skopje, Halkbank AD Skopje, ProCredit and Sparkasse Bank Makedonija - all deposit-takers doing online identification. [by the date of euro introduction, banks organise the loading...] — the denomination ladder is a CHANGEOVER-WINDOW loading rule that has now been spent, NOT a standing, permanently binding ATM denomination mandate. Within that window it required: ATMs with TWO note cassettes loaded with EUR 10 and EUR 20; THREE cassettes with EUR 10 and EUR 20, optionally also EUR 5 and/or EUR 50; FOUR cassettes with EUR 10 and EUR 20, optionally also EUR 5 and/or EUR 50 and/or EUR 100, where (a) withdrawals of EUR 100 or less must always be served from EUR 5, 10 and/or 20 and/or 50 notes and (b) only withdrawals above EUR 100 may use EUR 50 and/or EUR 100. What IS permanent is Art.18(2) (and ZVERB Art.23(6)): from the euro introduction date, cash withdrawals from self-service machines including ATMs dispense ONLY euro banknotes. Art.19: banks report frontloading/sub-frontloading data to BNB in the manner set by BNB instructions and must immediately notify any euro cash released into circulation early. Art.20: BNB may carry out on-site and documentary inspections of banks, their agents and sub-frontloaded merchants, each closing with a formal inspection protocol containing findings and/or recommendations. Sanctions for breaching ZVERB Art.23(6), verified in the statute: BGN 500–3,000 for individuals (Art.59(1)(4)) and a BGN 5,000–30,000 property sanction for legal persons, BGN 10,000–60,000 on repeat (Art.59(5)(11)). | [BNB Ordinance No.46 of 31/07/2025 (PDF, Bulgarian) — re-downloaded from bnb.bg and text-extracted locally; sanctions cross-checked in ZVERB Art.59](https://www.bnb.bg/bnbweb/groups/public/documents/bnb_law/regulations_46_bg.pdf) | 19/08/2025 | (a) WHO: every bank and independent deployer that ran an ATM through the Bulgarian changeover. NCR APTRA OptiCash re-parameterisation for the settled euro denomination mix, and multivendor withdrawal-menu logic, are normal refresh-cycle work. | Republic of Slovenia — Ministry of Finance (Ministrstvo za finance) with Banka Slovenije | Slovenia | The Ministry of Finance published the draft Act on the Accessibility and Use of Payment Instruments (SL: 'Zakon o dostopnosti in uporabi plačilnih sredstev', announced as 'predlog zakona o dostopnosti gotovine in brezplačnih osnovnih plačilnih storitvah') for PUBLIC CONSULTATION on 17/07/2026, closing 17/08/2026. It implements new Article 74.a of the Slovenian Constitution (right to use cash) and was prepared with Banka Slovenije. Confirmed parameters: credit institutions AND Pošta Slovenije must maintain a sufficient network of cash points (ATMs and branches) with adequate geographic coverage and density; settlements above 1,000 inhabitants must have an ATM within max 5 km of the settlement centre; at least 95% of the population must have cash access within 5 km by road; a national floor of at least 60 ATMs per 100,000 inhabitants; settlements above 3,000 inhabitants need a credit-institution or Pošta Slovenije branch within 5 km; branch density set in a 30–40 per 100,000 range with Banka Slovenije fixing the exact figure by secondary act; at most 3% of the required ATM density may be substituted by alternative cash points offering 'cash in shop' and 'cashback'. Transitional provision: at least 90% of the population within 5 km by 31/12/2029. Banka Slovenije gains power to ORDER a named credit institution by decision to install a cash point, with administrative sanctions for breach; Pošta Slovenije must apply to Banka Slovenije for a payment-services licence within 3 months of entry into force. The draft also mandates a free basic payment package (account, debit card, online/mobile banking, unlimited incoming transfers, own-bank cash transactions, at least FIVE free monthly withdrawals at OTHER banks' ATMs, card payments) for people receiving regular income, extended for vulnerable groups and pensioners; obliges merchants to accept cash with defined exceptions; and separately obliges providers of goods, services and digital content to enable payment by at least ONE electronic payment method. | [GOV.SI — Ministrstvo za finance, 'V javni obravnavi predlog zakona o dostopnosti gotovine in brezplačnih osnovnih plačilnih storitvah' ['Draft act on cash accessibility and free basic payment services in public consultation'], 17/07/2026; density/transition detail corroborated by Združenje mestnih občin Slovenije article summary and Lider.si analysis of the July text](https://www.gov.si/novice/2026-07-17-v-javni-obravnavi-predlog-zakona-o-dostopnosti-gotovine-in-brezplacnih-osnovnih-placilnih-storitvah/) | 17/07/2026 | (a) All Slovenian banks — NLB, NKBM (OTP), SKB (OTP), Intesa Sanpaolo Bank, Addiko, Gorenjska banka, Delavska hranilnica — plus Pošta Slovenije (which becomes a co-obliged party and must get a payment-services licence). Merchants and digital-content providers are separately hit by the accept-cash and accept-at-least-one-electronic-method duties. (b) HONEST READ: this is mainly a FLOOR that stops further ATM/branch rationalisation and forces geographic REDISTRIBUTION plus a coverage-reporting duty — it is not a large new-build programme. Slovenia already has 61.1 ATMs per 100,000 vs the 60 required (23 devices of headroom) and 38.1 branches per 100,000 against a 30–40 band, and the MoF's own economic-impact assessment states the criteria were calibrated to the current market and will not have major financial consequences for banks. The real Printec pull is therefore: coverage evidencing and reporting (ATM telemetry & monitoring as the natural evidence layer for a Banka Slovenije coverage duty), keeping mandated low-volume sites economic (cash recyclers — cash-in funds cash-out, halving CIT trips — Glory/Sesami TCRs and NCR recycling ATMs, plus APTRA OptiCash), x-core multivendor software so one estate can serve several brands including a Pošta Slovenije white-label, and the 'cash in shop'/'cashback' alternative-cash-point substitution (up to 3% of density), which is a merchant-side POS/kiosk integration play. The five free out-of-network withdrawals per customer per month genuinely erode interchange economics on those sites, which strengthens the cost-per-cash-point argument. (c) Consultation closes 17/08/2026; the binding geographic transitional date is 31/12/2029, so the network build/redistribution budget is 2027–2029, not H1-2027. Banks that want to shape the coverage map and the Banka Slovenije secondary act on branch density must engage NOW. | High that a cash-access law passes — it implements a constitutional article. | National Bank of Ukraine (NBU) — all Ukrainian banks, PSPs, commercial agents and self-service terminal operators | Ukraine | NBU Board Resolution No. 67 of 16/06/2026 ('On approving Amendments to certain regulatory acts of the National Bank of Ukraine on cash circulation'), officially published 18/06/2026 and IN FORCE FROM 26/06/2026, amends the Instruction on the organisation of cash work (Resolution No. 103 of 25/09/2018) and the Rules on determining payment features and exchange of banknotes (Resolution No. 134 of 03/12/2018). It replaces points 124–124(3) of Section VI with TEN new points 124–124(9). Point 124(8) makes the payer's FULL mobile number in +380-XX-XXXXXXX format a mandatory receipt field for cash-in via a PTKS (self-service payment terminal). Point 124(1) obliges the PSP/commercial agent to send a one-time password (OTP) to that number by SMS or read it out by automated phone call. Point 124(2) requires — REGARDLESS OF THE TRANSFER AMOUNT — that the payer key their full mobile number on the PTKS screen and then key the received OTP on the PTKS screen. Point 124(3) requires PSPs, including where commercial agents are used, to take CONTINUOUS measures to prevent the OTP being passed to another person, including shortening its validity. Point 124(4) CARVES OUT: payments of taxes, fines, administrative-service fees, duties and other budget payments, and payments under the Housing and Communal Services Act plus fixed telephony, TV and internet. Point 124(5) BANS the PSP/agent from executing the payment operation at all if points 124–124(4) are not met. Points 124(6)–124(7) additionally require PSPs to RETAIN the receipts and to retain transaction information in the operational-accounting system for production to the NBU on supervisory demand. The same resolution inserts a NEW Ukrainian licence category, 'bank of financial inclusion' (UA: 'банк фінансової інклюзії'), operating under a LIMITED banking licence, into the cash-work Instruction. | [National Bank of Ukraine — Постанова Правління НБУ № 67 від 16 червня 2026 року (PDF on bank.gov.ua), full text extracted locally ['On approving Amendments to certain NBU regulatory acts on cash circulation']](https://bank.gov.ua/admin_uploads/law/16062026_67.pdf?v=19) | 26/06/2026 | (a) Every Ukrainian bank and non-bank PSP running self-service cash-in terminals and cash-accepting ATMs — PrivatBank, Oschadbank, Raiffeisen Bank Ukraine, Universal Bank/monobank, PUMB — plus the large independent terminal networks (EasyPay, City24, iBox) and their commercial agents. (b) Every cash-in terminal application needs a NEW screen flow (phone-number entry, OTP entry, retry/expiry handling), a new receipt template carrying the full mobile number, an SMS/voice-call OTP delivery integration, per-payment-type branching to honour the budget-payment and utilities carve-outs, a hard block that refuses the transaction when the flow is not completed, and receipt/transaction ARCHIVING retrievable on NBU demand. That is a multivendor self-service SOFTWARE and kiosk-application change plus an archiving change — not a hardware change. Printec map: x-core multivendor ATM/kiosk software and screen-flow customisation, self-service and bank kiosks, ATM/device telemetry to prove the new flow is live estate-wide, DOCUMENT MANAGEMENT/archiving for the new receipt and transaction retention duty, and AML/transaction monitoring (IMTF Siron, FICO, INETCO) to detect the OTP-sharing and cash-mule patterns the NBU is explicitly targeting. The 'bank of financial inclusion' limited licence is a NEW class of small institution that will need a turnkey, low-cost channel stack. (c) In force since 26/06/2026 — this is remediation spend NOW, and any operator still non-compliant is an immediate opportunity, sharpened by the point-124(5) prohibition on executing non-compliant transactions. | Certain — in force | Medium | M | Medium | Map which Ukrainian terminal networks have actually shipped the OTP flow (spot-check operator app-release notes and terminal screens) to find the laggards. Pull the NBU licensing regulation for the 'bank of financial inclusion' to size that new segment. DATA PROTECTION: this mandates capturing a mobile number, printing the FULL number on a receipt, and retaining receipts — do not advise a customer on the Ukrainian data-protection or GDPR exposure of that; state the NBU text and refer the question to Printec's DPO / Compliance team. |
| Finanzmarktaufsicht (FMA) Austria — banks, payment institutions, crypto-asset service providers and insurers | Austria | From 01/01/2026 the FMA is the supervisory authority for monitoring and enforcing FINANCIAL SANCTIONS in the Austrian financial sector, taking the function over from the Oesterreichische Nationalbank (OeNB) and completing a reform decided in 2024 (the transfer of competences from OeNB to FMA was enacted by the first legislative act of the new Nationalrat). Announced by the FMA on 30/12/2025. ALL financial-market participants — banks, PAYMENT INSTITUTIONS, crypto-asset service providers and insurance undertakings — are now within the scope of the Sanktionengesetz and under FMA supervision for it. They must establish strategies, controls and procedures to mitigate the risk of non-compliance with AND CIRCUMVENTION of sanctions, and sanctions-related REPORTS must in future be addressed to the FMA (not the OeNB). The FMA now combines AML/CFT supervision and financial-sanctions supervision in one authority, describing the result as a one-stop shop with harmonised processes and coordinated audit cycles. | [FMA Österreich — 'FMA ist ab 1. Jänner 2026 die neue Aufsichtsbehörde zur Überwachung und Durchsetzung von Finanzsanktionen' (German original; English version 'From 1 January 2026, the FMA is the new supervisory authority for monitoring and imposing financial sanctions'), distributed via APA-OTS on 30/12/2025](https://www.fma.gv.at/fma-ist-ab-1-jaenner-2026-die-neue-aufsichtsbehoerde-zur-ueberwachung-und-durchsetzung-von-finanzsanktionen/) | 01/01/2026 | (a) All Austrian banks (Erste Group, Raiffeisen Bank International and the Raiffeisen sector, UniCredit Bank Austria, BAWAG, Volksbanken, Oberbank), payment institutions, CASPs and insurers — and, through group supervision, RBI's and Erste's CEE subsidiaries across the footprint. (b) A single authority running combined AML + sanctions audit cycles raises the evidentiary bar on SANCTIONS SCREENING specifically: real-time and batch screening of customers, counterparties and payment messages, list-management currency, alert audit trails, and testing/tuning documentation. The explicit CIRCUMVENTION limb is the sharpest new hook — detecting evasion typologies is a transaction-monitoring and network-analytics problem, not a list-matching one. The redirection of sanctions REPORTING to the FMA is a concrete workflow and template change. Printec map: IMTF Siron (sanctions/embargo and AML screening — already a footprint reference at Alpha Bank), FICO and INETCO transaction monitoring, plus document management for the audit evidence pack and the new reporting trail. | European Banking Authority (EBA) — deposit guarantee schemes and all deposit-taking banks | EU-wide footprint (AT, BG, HR, CY, CZ, GR, HU, RO, SK, SI) | On 23/07/2026 the EBA launched its FIRST FOUR consultations under the revised Deposit Guarantee Schemes Directive (DGSD3), out of 12 mandated technical standards and guidelines: (1) ITS on depositor information (mandate under Article 16 DGSD3) — harmonised content and format of the depositor information sheet at account opening and periodically, plus required communications in specific situations such as mergers and failures; (2) ITS on information exchange between credit institutions, DGSs and other relevant authorities — standardised procedures, templates and minimum requirements for bank-failure scenarios; (3) RTS on the treatment/protection of client funds held in intermediary accounts, so DGSs can identify and reimburse underlying clients; (4) Guidelines on the investment of DGS available financial means. | PSR Articles 85a and 108a — provisions applying from day one | EU-wide | MATERIAL CORRECTION TO THE RESEARCHER'S READING OF ARTICLE 85a. Article 112 does carve two provisions out of the 21-month clock and applies them FROM THE DATE OF ENTRY INTO FORCE (20 days after OJ publication) — that part is confirmed. But Article 85a 'Strong customer authentication in respect of credit transfers' is NARROWER than reported: the derogation from Article 85(1)(c) applies to recurring credit transfers 'initiated by the payer's payment service provider' following a request from the PAYEE — not, as the researcher wrote, 'initiated by the payer' — and only where THREE CUMULATIVE conditions are met: (a) the transfers are initiated by the payer's PSP following a request from the payee; (b) the payee's request rests on an agreement between payer and payee setting out frequency and amounts, including the circumstances in which amounts may vary; and (c) the payer agrees that its PSP executes the transfers in line with that agreement. Article 108a is a transitional provision which, by derogation from PSD2 (Directive (EU) 2015/2366) and until 21 months after entry into force, excludes certain payment transactions in electronic money tokens and certain crypto-asset service provider transactions from PSD2 — i.e. it is a crypto/e-money-token carve-out, not a general transitional rule. | [Council of the EU, doc ST-8221-2026-INIT (PSR final compromise text) — Articles 85a, 108a, 112](https://data.consilium.europa.eu/doc/document/ST-8221-2026-INIT/en/pdf) | 17/04/2026 | (a) Affected: footprint EU PSPs running payee-requested recurring credit transfers under a standing payer-payee agreement — a narrower population than all standing orders. (b) Must buy/change: the Art 85a derogation REDUCES friction, letting banks drop repeat SCA on qualifying recurring credit transfers, but only where the three cumulative conditions are evidenced — which means the authentication engine must carry the payer-payee agreement terms (frequency, amount, variation circumstances) as decisioning data, not just a flag. That is a controlled change to the SCA decisioning layer plus a mandate-data model. It lands on day one of entry into force with no 21-month grace. (c) Budget: small, and it lands the moment the OJ appears — the earliest PSR-driven change request Printec can raise with clients, and a low-risk door-opener into the larger Article 59 conversation. Art 108a carries no Printec product consequence and should not be sold. | High | Medium | S | Medium | Use the corrected Art 85a scope in client conversations — the derogation is payee-requested/PSP-initiated and condition-bound, so do not tell clients they can drop SCA on standing orders generally. |
| National Bank of Ukraine (NBU) — open banking framework | Ukraine | [STANDING — dated 2025] Ukraine has a PSD2-equivalent open-banking regime already in force, ahead of the EU's PSD3 cycle. Confirmed on the NBU's own Open Banking page: the Regulation on Open Banking in Ukraine (NBU Board Resolution No. 80 of 25/07/2025); the Regulation on the Authorization Procedure for Providers of Non-financial Payment Services (Resolution No. 81 of 25/07/2025); and the Regulation on the Procedure for Using Electronic Trust Services when PSPs Obtain Access to Users' Accounts (Resolution No. 82 of 25/07/2025). The same page also links the Regulation on Liability Insurance of Non-financial Payment Service Providers (Resolution No. 71 of 01/07/2025) and the Regulation on Risk Management System Requirements for Non-financial Payment Service Providers (Resolution No. 73 of 02/07/2025). ASPSPs (banks) must set up specialised interfaces (APIs), ensure continuous real-time TPP (PISP/AISP) access to users' accounts, and publish each API's technical specifications on their own websites. TPPs must hold a qualified open-banking certificate from a qualified provider of electronic trust services (QPETS). SCA is governed separately by NBU Resolution No. 58 of 03/05/2023 and ICT security by Resolution No. 43 of 19/05/2021. The framework entered into force 01/08/2025. PRESS ONLY, NOT CONFIRMED ON THE NBU PAGE: reports (The Paypers, Asters) that banks had to comply by 01/01/2026 and that NBU Board Resolution No. 32 of 31/03/2026 simplified PISP payment-initiation detail requirements. | [National Bank of Ukraine — Open Banking (official page; retrieved with a desktop User-Agent after an initial 403)](https://bank.gov.ua/en/payments/open-banking) | 25/07/2025 | (a) Affected: all Ukrainian ASPSPs including PrivatBank (existing Printec NCR recycler customer) and Oschadbank. (b) Must buy/change: Ukrainian banks already carry a live API-availability, published-specification and qualified-certificate obligation — the same technical surface the EU's PSR Article 38 will impose. Printec can sell API availability monitoring and electronic-trust-services integration in Ukraine TODAY on an in-force rule, rather than on a 2028 EU deadline — a rare case where the non-EU footprint is the nearer-term buyer. (c) Budget: already committed; the buying window is remediation and monitoring, not greenfield build. | High | Medium | S | Medium | Confirm NBU Resolution No. 32/2026 and the 01/01/2026 bank compliance date directly on bank.gov.ua's Ukrainian-language legislation register — both remain press-only and must not be restated as fact without the primary text. |

The CPMI/BIS Task Team 3 follow-up report (February 2026) places AIPS in the Europe section, notes it 'is expected to implement the ISO 20022 messaging standard in the coming years' and that since AIPS intends to adopt usage guidelines aligned with HVPS+ (High Value Payments Plus, the global market practice for RTGS messages) the assessment uses HVPS+ as reference. On requirement 11 (postal address) Albania is 'Scheduled 2027': 'Given the limited number of cross-border payments processed in the RTGS system and the need to ensure operational stability following the recent migration, the Bank of Albania plans to align with the hybrid postal address requirement as part of the IAP version upgrade scheduled for November 2027.' Ziro kliring handles interbank transactions of 10,000.00 convertible marks (BAM) or less, settled the same day in the netting cycle to which they were submitted, across three branches (Sarajevo, Mostar, Banja Luka) plus a central Ziro kliring centar; the RTGS handles amounts above BAM 10,000. Combined with live IPS BiH (TIPS clone) traffic since 20/07/2026, that is a concentrated data-remediation and message-mapping programme in a small market: document management for remediation, Namirial eKYC for structured capture, INETCO transaction monitoring across Ziro kliring / RTGS / IPS, Siron and FICO screening on structured party fields, plus integration and managed services. | Ministry of Digital Transformation / Sobranie — North Macedonia | North Macedonia | [STANDING — dated 2025] North Macedonia legislated accessibility in 2025 but ONLY on the public-sector web side, not the EAA product side: the Law on Accessibility of Websites and Mobile Applications of Public Sector Institutions was published in Службен весник на Република Северна Македонија No.198 of 18/10/2025 (proposed to parliament by the Ministry of Digital Transformation on 12/09/2025). It requires public-sector content to be perceivable through at least two different senses, all interactive elements operable regardless of input method, and content technically robust and compatible with assistive technologies, and Art.6 requires each institution to publish an accessibility statement — i.e. a transposition of the WEB ACCESSIBILITY DIRECTIVE (Directive (EU) 2016/2102), NOT of the European Accessibility Act. Banks, ATMs, payment terminals and consumer banking services are therefore OUT of scope; no North Macedonian law imposes EN 301 549-style conformity on self-service terminals and no market-surveillance authority has been designated for them. NBRSM has published no accessibility requirement for ATM or POS estates identifiable from primary sources as of 03/08/2026. | [Ministry of Digital Transformation of the Republic of North Macedonia, press release on the law making public-institution websites and mobile applications more accessible to persons with disabilities (Службен весник No.198, 18/10/2025)](https://mdt.gov.mk/mk-MK/odnosi-so-javnost/soopstenija/vardziski-so-novo-zakonsko-resenie-veb-stranicite-i-mobilni-aplikacii-na-javnite-institucii-stanuvaat-popristapni-za-licata-so-poprecenost) | 18/10/2025 | (a) AFFECTED: Komercijalna banka Skopje, NLB Banka Skopje, Stopanska banka (OTP), Halkbank, ProCredit, Sparkasse Banka Makedonija — none currently under an EAA-equivalent terminal obligation. | Central Bank of the Republic of Kosovo (CBK/BQK) | Kosovo | RE-VERIFIED VERBATIM FROM THE CBK PDF - AND STRONGER THAN FIRST REPORTED. The CBK Board approved on 29/08/2025 the 'Regulation on Licensing of Crypto-Assets Service Operators Providing Crypto-Assets Exchange Services', made under Law No. 03/L-209 on the CBK and Law No. 08/L-295 on Crypto-Assets. Article 23: it enters into force 90 days after Board approval; Article 21: institutions subject to licensing must within 90 days of entry into force take all necessary actions to apply and comply - so the application window ran to roughly late February 2026. Article 1.1 expressly brings CRYPTO ATMs ('including crypto ATMs or cryptocurrency ATMs') with a physical office base inside the licensing perimeter, and the CASO definition repeats this. CRITICAL ADDITIONAL FINDINGS not previously captured: CASOs offering services through ATMs MUST OBTAIN AN ADDITIONAL LICENCE FROM THE CBK for that activity; CASOs conducting cash operations must engage one or more licensed CASH-IN-TRANSIT (CIT) insurance companies under the Regulation on minimum security requirements; the CBK will set MAXIMUM CASH LIMITS held in ATMs and in internal vaults together with the relevant security requirements; and CASOs must publish their prices, costs and fees visibly in offices, AT THE ATMs, and on their website. Article 22: breaches attract corrective measures and administrative penalties under Art.67 of Law 03/L-209 and Law 08/L-295. | [CBK/BQK - Regulation on Licensing of Crypto-Assets Service Operators Providing Crypto-Assets Exchange Services (EN PDF, downloaded with desktop UA and extracted locally)](https://bqk-kos.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/09/ENG-Rregullore-per-licencimin-e-operatoreve-te-kripto-aseteve.pdf?lang=en) | 29/08/2025 | (a) WHO: Kosovo crypto exchange operators and crypto-ATM deployers holding or seeking a CBK licence, plus the Kosovo banks that will bank them (Raiffeisen Kosovo, ProCredit, NLB, TEB). (b) WHAT: Kosovo is the ONLY footprint market that explicitly licenses crypto ATMs - and the regulation goes further than a licensing perimeter, mandating an additional CBK licence per ATM activity, licensed cash-in-transit arrangements, CBK-set cash limits in ATMs and vaults, security requirements, and fee display at the machine.
