| Central Bank of Montenegro (CBCG) and Central Bank of Kosovo (CBK); Montenegrin and Kosovar banks | Montenegro | [STANDING — long-running, with a live 2026 accession angle] Montenegro and Kosovo use the euro UNILATERALLY ('euroisation') without being euro-area members. CBCG's official euroisation page records the path — Deutsche Mark introduced as a parallel currency on 2 November 1999, sole legal tender from 1 January 2001, replaced by the euro in March 2002 — and states that Montenegro is 'treated as a specific case since Montenegro entered this regime before ECOFIN Council adopted the position prohibiting unilateral euroisation'. The page records the consequences: only a limited possibility of independent monetary policy (it imports ECB policy and cannot set reference rates or control money supply), no access to the emergency liquidity assistance available to EU central banks, and effectively no seigniorage. CORRECTION TO THE 'NO BACKSTOP' READING: the CBCG does hold two limited euro backstops — an ECB precautionary repo line (EUREP) of up to EUR 250 million and a renewable EUR 100 million credit line from the Bank for International Settlements. Euroisation falls under Chapter 17 (Economic and Monetary Policy) of Montenegro's accession negotiations, opened in 2018, and Montenegro has said it will seek to retain the euro. Montenegro is the accession frontrunner and aims to conclude negotiations by end-2026, but as at March 2026 it had provisionally closed 14 of 33 chapters — the pace, not the ambition, is the binding constraint. The ECB's standing position remains that unilateral euroisation is not compatible with the Treaty and cannot bypass the convergence process. Kosovo's position mirrors Montenegro's. Any accession-treaty formula specifically recognising Montenegro's regime is press-level and not confirmed in an EU primary document. | [Central Bank of Montenegro (CBCG) — 'Euroisation' official page; Council of the EU / EEAS releases on provisional chapter closures; bankar.me and investitor.me (press) on Chapter 17](https://www.cbcg.me/en/core-functions/monetary-policy/euroisation) | 03/08/2026 | (a) WHO: all Montenegrin banks and the Kosovar banks — both markets already run euro cash end to end. PCI DSS v3.2.1 was retired 31/03/2024 and the future-dated v4.x requirements became effective 31/03/2025, so EVERY requirement is in scope for assessments performed during 2026 — there is no separate new 2026 core deadline. The PCI SSC ran a six-week request-for-comments period on PCI DSS v4.0.1 from 03/06/2026 to 20/07/2026, explicitly to begin shaping the NEXT iteration of the standard (soliciting input on implementation, strengths, future technology and AI innovation); the RFC is not a delay or safe harbour for missed v4.0.1 controls. | Digital euro services by PSPs in non-euro-area Member States — Regulation 2023/0211(COD) / COM(2023) 368 | Romania, Czech Republic, Hungary (non-euro EU footprint) | [STANDING — dated to the 2023 Commission proposal.] The non-euro-area arrangements sit in a SEPARATE regulation from the Digital Euro Regulation itself: procedure 2023/0211(COD), COM(2023) 368, on the provision of digital euro services by payment service providers incorporated in Member States whose currency is not the euro. Per the European Parliament's own document summary of 28/06/2023, PSPs incorporated in non-euro-area Member States 'should also...be in a position to distribute the digital euro', applying 'the same rules as payment services providers incorporated in Member States whose currency is the euro for the purposes of distributing the digital euro', with 'the supervision and enforcement of the obligations by the Member States whose currency is not the euro'. Access extends to persons 'residing or established in Member States whose currency is not the euro and in other countries of the European Economic Area or any third countries'. The further condition the researcher stated — that third-country access requires a prior EU agreement and/or arrangements concluded between the ECB and the relevant national central bank — could NOT be found in this summary and is unverified. Romania's own euro-adoption target (ERM II 2026, euro 2029) is widely assessed as unrealistic given the fiscal deficit; Czechia and Hungary have no adoption date. | [European Parliament OEIL document summary, procedure 2023/0211(COD) — provision of digital euro services by PSPs incorporated in Member States whose currency is not the euro](https://oeil.europarl.europa.eu/oeil/en/document-summary?id=1749394) | 28/06/2023 | (a) WHO: BCR, BRD, Banca Transilvania, Raiffeisen Romania (RO); ČSOB, Komerční banka, Česká spořitelna (CZ); OTP, K&H, Erste Hungary (HU) — all of whom serve euro-area customers or belong to euro-area groups. (b) WHAT: no mandatory acceptance obligation in RO, CZ or HU, but banks there that want to distribute the digital euro cross-border, or whose euro-area parents standardise a group-wide stack, will still need certified acceptance and wallet components. Realistically a follow-on wave, not a 2027 spend. Note the practical consequence of the separate-regulation point: this file can be adopted, amended or delayed on a different timetable from the main Digital Euro Regulation, so RO/CZ/HU account teams should track 2023/0211(COD) specifically rather than assuming the headline digital euro dates apply to them. (c) WHEN: post-2029. | National Bank of Ukraine (NBU) — e-hryvnia | Ukraine | [STANDING — NBU e-hryvnia page as retrieved 03/08/2026; project launched September 2021.] The NBU's official e-hryvnia page confirms the project remains at research and design stage with NO pilot date and no launch timeline published. The NBU 'is considering and processing the following possible options for using the e-hryvnia that will shape its design and main features': the e-hryvnia for retail noncash payments with options for 'programmed' targeted money; the e-hryvnia in the circulation of virtual assets; and the e-hryvnia for cross-border payments. Stated design features, verbatim from the page: wallet access restoration using DLT (distributed ledger technology), so that if the user's financial institution cannot provide access 'the user will only need to restore access to their wallet through another financial institution'; 'offline payments is the option to deliver payments if either the payer or the payee does not have a connection to the e-hryvnia platform, and the option of delivering payments if both the payer and the payee do not have an immediate connection is also being considered'; programmability including split payments, conditional payments and recurring payments; and 'the option to provide targeted government aid, regardless of merchants and payment service providers'. The term e-hryvnia is defined in the Law of Ukraine On Payment Services; if the decision to introduce it is approved it would be legal tender and exist only in cashless form. Published artefacts are the Analytical Report on the E-hryvnia Pilot Project, the CBDCinUA conference report and a survey of financial market experts on the possibility of e-hryvnia implementation. | European Parliament ECON committee chair vs. EP Legislative Observatory forecast | EU | CONFLICTING TIMING SIGNALS, BOTH TRACEABLE — BUT OF DIFFERENT EVIDENTIAL WEIGHT. On 18/05/2026 the chair of the EP ECON committee reportedly wrote to the Council Presidency confirming that if the Council transmits its position on PSD3 and the PSR in its current form, ECON will recommend adoption at second reading WITHOUT amendment, and urged acceleration of the legal-linguistic finalisation so Parliament can adopt the package 'by September 2026 at the latest'. This is reported consistently by multiple law firms but the letter itself is not published as a retrievable primary document — it is PRESS-TIER evidence. Against it stand two PRIMARY sources: the EP Legislative Observatory still lists the file as 'Awaiting Council's 1st reading position' with an indicative plenary sitting date of 14/12/2026, and the EP Legislative Train entry (last updated 20/06/2026) says only that 'the deal needs to be formally adopted by Parliament and Council before it can enter into force', giving no dates. The binding constraint is legal-linguistic revision of two texts running to roughly 431 and 167 pages in 24 languages. As of 03/08/2026 the September-2026 target looks missed: no Council 1st-reading position has been transmitted. | [EP Legislative Train Schedule — payment services package (primary, last update 20/06/2026); EP Legislative Observatory 2023/0210(COD) (primary); Bird & Bird, Mamo TCV and Norton Rose Fulbright notes on the 18/05/2026 ECON chair letter (press)](https://www.europarl.europa.eu/legislative-train/theme-an-economy-that-works-for-people/file-revision-of-eu-rules-on-payment-services) | 20/06/2026 | (a) Affected: Printec's own FY2027 sales planning more than any single bank. (b) The spread between a September-2026 and a December-2026 plenary is a full quarter on every downstream date — general application moves between roughly Q3 2028 and Q1 2029, and the VoP tail between Q1 2029 and Q3 2029. (c) Budget: plan on the LATER date for pipeline forecasting and the EARLIER date for solution readiness, and do not publish a specific application date in customer-facing material until the OJ L number exists. | Medium that plenary adoption occurs in Q4 2026; the September-2026 target had already slipped as at 03/08/2026 in the sense that no Council 1st-reading position had been transmitted | Medium | Unscoped | — | The single highest-value watch item on this beat: the Council's first-reading position. | ETSI/CEN/CENELEC — EN 301 549, the harmonised standard for the EAA | EU-wide (footprint: AT, BG, HR, CY, CZ, EL, HU, RO, SK, SI) | MATERIAL CORRECTION OF A WIDELY REPEATED ASSUMPTION, re-verified from the ETSI deliverable itself. The version of EN 301 549 currently in force, V3.2.1 (March 2021), was prepared under standardisation request C(2017)2585 and maps only to the WEB ACCESSIBILITY DIRECTIVE (Directive (EU) 2016/2102) — it is NOT cited in the Official Journal under the European Accessibility Act. The first version prepared under the EAA standardisation request M/587 (Commission Implementing Decision C(2022)6456 final) is Draft EN 301 549 V4.1.0 (2025-11), which adds a new Annex ZB mapping to Directive (EU) 2019/882 and a new clause A.2 allowing conformance of specific ICT products and services to be evaluated against the EAA essential requirements. Its own Foreword states the presumption of conformity arises only 'once the present document is cited in the Official Journal of the European Union under that Directive'. As of 03/08/2026 V4.1.0 remains in the combined Public Enquiry and Vote phase of ETSI's Standardisation Request deliverable Approval Procedure (SRdAP); the final version and the OJ citation had not issued. Content changes: clauses 9, 10 and 11 realigned to WCAG 2.2, and real-time-text requirements in clause 6.2 significantly extended to total conversation. National transposition dates are relative, not absolute: announcement (doa) 3 months after ETSI publication, national publication/endorsement (dop/e) 6 months after doa, withdrawal of conflicting national standards (dow) 18 months after doa. | [ETSI/CEN/CENELEC, Draft EN 301 549 V4.1.0 (2025-11), Harmonised European Standard 'Accessibility requirements for ICT products and services' — PDF downloaded from the ETSI deliver repository and text-extracted locally (Foreword and Introduction)](https://www.etsi.org/deliver/etsi_en/301500_301599/301549/04.01.00_20/en_301549v040100ev.pdf) | Nov-2025 | (a) AFFECTED: every bank, acquirer and terminal deployer in the 10 EU footprint states, and Printec itself as an economic operator placing ATMs and POS terminals on those markets. (b) MUST BUY/CHANGE: because no harmonised standard is yet cited in the OJ for the EAA, a supplier CANNOT today claim automatic presumption of conformity by invoking EN 301 549 — conformity must be self-declared against the Annex I essential requirements with its own technical documentation and EU declaration of conformity. That makes documented, test-evidenced accessibility conformance a genuine differentiator in ATM/POS tenders rather than a tick-box. Maps to Printec NCR ATM supply with documented conformity files, x-core multivendor accessible UI (the cheapest way to bring a mixed legacy fleet to one evidenced baseline), Verifone/Castles accessible POS + TMS, and accessibility gap-assessment services. (c) BUDGET: when the final version is cited in the OJ, the WCAG 2.2 uplift in clauses 9-11 will re-open every bank's digital-channel and terminal-UI conformance evidence — banks should fund a re-assessment in the 2027 budget, decided H2-2026, and should require suppliers to commit contractually to the V4 series rather than V3.2.1. | Medium-High that the final version is approved and cited in the OJ within 12 months; the vote phase is the last procedural step | Medium | Unscoped | — | CLOSES the baseline's loose statement that EAA conformance runs 'via EN 301 549 (incl. | Czechia, Hungary, Poland, Romania, Sweden — the five EU Member States with a derogation (footprint: CZ, HU, RO) | Czech Republic | The ECB and the European Commission published their biennial CONVERGENCE REPORTS on 24/06/2026, assessing Czechia, Hungary, Poland, Romania and Sweden. Bulgaria is not assessed: it requested an ad hoc convergence assessment on 25/02/2025, the Council decided in July 2025 that it fulfilled the conditions, and it adopted the euro on 01/01/2026. Headline findings, re-verified against both the ECB release and Commission Institutional Paper 343: none of the five currencies participates in ERM II (the exchange rate mechanism a country must sit in for at least two years before euro adoption), so on the fastest conceivable path none can adopt the euro before roughly 2029–2030. Price stability reference value 2.7% (average of Cyprus 0.9%, France 1.2%, Denmark 1.6% plus 1.5pp), reference period June 2025–May 2026: Czechia 1.9% (passes), Hungary 3.3% (fails), Romania 8.4% (fails), Poland fails, Sweden passes. Long-term interest-rate reference value 5.1%: Czechia 4.5% and Sweden 2.6% pass; Poland 5.4%, Hungary 6.7% and Romania 6.7% fail. Hungary, Poland and Romania all breached the 3% deficit limit in 2025; Romania has been in the Excessive Deficit Procedure since 2020 with a corrective deadline of 2030, Hungary and Poland entered EDP in July 2024 with deadlines of 2026 and 2028. None of the five is fully compatible with the Treaty and the ESCB/ECB Statute on legal convergence — including Czechia, which otherwise meets three of the four economic criteria and fails only the exchange-rate criterion by not being in ERM II. The previous regular reports were adopted in June 2024, so the next scheduled biennial assessment is 2028 unless a Member State requests an ad hoc report as Bulgaria did. | [ECB press release 'ECB reports on progress towards euro adoption' (pr260624) + European Commission Convergence Report 2026, Institutional Paper 343 (PDF re-downloaded and text-extracted)](https://www.ecb.europa.eu/press/pr/date/2026/html/ecb.pr260624~ec1dcac037.en.html) | 24/06/2026 | (a) WHO: Czech, Hungarian and Romanian banks — Printec's CZ/HU/RO exposure. No CZ/HU/RO euro-changeover tender (dual-currency ATM/POS software, frontloading logistics, cassette/denomination re-fit, cash-forecast rebuild) can credibly be issued in the next 18 months because none of the three is in ERM II. CZ/HU/RO demand in 2026–27 remains driven by instant payments and VoP (Verification of Payee — the payer's bank must check the payee name matches the IBAN before the transfer is confirmed, with a 09/07/2027 deadline for non-euro-area Member States), accessibility/EAA and DORA — not euro adoption. (c) WHEN: no changeover budget to chase. Following the Tisza party's 12/04/2026 election win, Finance Minister András Kármán committed Hungary to MEETING THE MAASTRICHT CRITERIA BY 2030 — not to adopting the euro in 2030 — and said at a Budapest press conference in June 2026 that the government will submit a REVISED MEDIUM-TERM FISCAL-STRUCTURAL PLAN later in 2026 setting out the path, with the anchor being a general-government deficit below 3% of GDP. Hungarian coverage of the same statement notes that, because ERM II membership requires a minimum two years, the earliest conceivable euro introduction on this path is around 2031, and that ERM II entry 'does not have to happen immediately' and would be a joint decision with the MNB. | CBBH (Bosnia & Herzegovina), NBS (Serbia), Bank of Albania, NBRNM (North Macedonia), NBU (Ukraine) | Bosnia & Herzegovina | [STANDING — context] The remaining non-EU footprint markets have NO euro-adoption pipeline and no changeover deadlines. Bosnia & Herzegovina runs a currency board with the convertible mark pegged to the euro — the CBBH's own site publishes EUR 1.95583, and states that it maintains monetary stability by issuing domestic currency under the currency board arrangement. That is numerically the SAME rate at which Bulgaria's lev converted, a legacy of both pegs having been set against the Deutsche Mark, so a future BiH changeover would be arithmetically trivial — but it is not on any official timetable and BiH is not an EU member. Serbia, Albania, North Macedonia and Ukraine retain their own currencies (dinar, lek, denar, hryvnia) with no ERM II perspective; their euro-related alignment runs through SEPA accession and payment-system harmonisation, not currency substitution. (c) WHEN: n/a — explicitly de-prioritise euro-changeover messaging in these five markets. | No euro adoption in any of these five within the planning horizon | Medium | Unscoped | — | Do not re-research euro adoption for BA/RS/AL/MK/UA each run; revisit only on an ERM II event or an EU accession-treaty monetary-chapter development. If BiH's peg is ever quoted in customer material, cite the CBBH page, not a third party. |
| European Commission DG FISMA - MiCA review consultation | EU (all 10 EU footprint states) | CONFIRMED ON THE COMMISSION'S OWN PAGE. The European Commission opened a targeted consultation on the review of the MiCA Regulation on 20/05/2026, closing 30/09/2026 at 23:59 CEST. It feeds the report mandated by MiCA Articles 140 and 142 and asks whether MiCA 'remains fit for purpose in light of the initial implementation and market and policy developments'. The stated target audience is digital-assets industry representatives (CASPs, crypto-asset issuers) and public authorities including national and European supervisors, central banks and finance ministries. NOTE: the specific live topics attributed to this consultation in the earlier draft - centralising CASP supervision at ESMA, EMT/ART treatment, third-country multi-issuer stablecoin schemes - and the claim of a June 2027 third-country equivalence report under Art.140(2)(v) are NOT stated on the consultation landing page and remain unverified. | [European Commission - Targeted consultation on the review of the MiCA Regulation](https://finance.ec.europa.eu/regulation-and-supervision/consultations-0/targeted-consultation-review-mica-regulation_en) | 20/05/2026 | (a) WHO: every footprint bank or CASP building crypto capability, and Printec's own product roadmap. (b) WHAT: a 'MiCA-2' proposal is realistically 2027, meaning a second compliance cycle - so argue for configurable, rule-driven AML and monitoring platforms (Siron, INETCO, FICO) and modular HSM key management over hard-coded builds, on the explicit ground that the rulebook is already under review. (c) WHEN: no procurement deadline, but Printec and its bank customers can respond until 30/09/2026, and this shapes 2027-2028 budgets. | High that a legislative proposal follows in 2027 | Medium | Unscoped | — | Read the consultation document PDF (finance.ec.europa.eu/document/download/62be7015-f066-4fac-b74e-71bacdbcc9f5_en) before asserting anything about ESMA supervision centralisation or a June 2027 equivalence report - neither is supported by the landing page, and both were carried in the draft without a source. |

### The five dates that should drive Q3-2026 action

1. **31/10/2026 — ECB SSM-2026-0301 action plans.** The only item where a supervisor has ordered every
   significant institution in the euro-area footprint to write down its end-of-life technology and a
   remediation timeline, on a fixed date, with horizontal ECB follow-up. Plans are drafted Aug-Oct 2026.
2. **17/08/2026 — Slovenia's cash-accessibility consultation closes.** A draft ATM density floor
   (60 per 100,000 inhabitants, 5 km coverage) is a legislated fleet-EXPANSION mandate — rare, and
   still open to comment.
3. **31/12/2026 — Hungary's statutory rural ATM mandate.** Nine named banks, machines that cannot use
   the EAA legacy derogation. Confirm the phase date against the decree before quoting the timeline.
4. **15/11/2026 — the EPC address cut-off.** The deadline MOVED from 22/11 to 15/11, and Hungary's VIBER
   lacks hybrid-address support with no confirmed timeline. Both facts create remediation conversations.
5. **01/01/2027 — Bulgaria's instant-payments deadline.** Potentially 12 months earlier than the baseline
   assumed. Verify the legal basis first — it currently rests on a press-quoted official, not a published rule.

Items below appear in the table but could NOT be confirmed this run and must not be quoted to customers as
fact: the Cyprus EAA transposition; the Cypriot VoP go-live; the Austrian and Croatian EUDI-wallet positions;
and the DORA subcontracting RTS (EUR-Lex blocked to the fetch tool). Several eIDAS national-status rows are
press-tier and explicitly tagged as not re-verified.

For any question touching data-subject rights, a personal-data breach, or a contested reading of a
regulation, refer to Printec's DPO / Compliance team rather than relying on this file.

## Verification notes
- **Corrected/contradicted by the adversarial pass (8):** Central Bank of the Republic of Kosovo (BQK) — MATERIAL DATE ERROR FOUND. The researcher assigned source_date 20/07/2026 to this BQK URL and wrote that BQK 'has concluded its SEPA pre-application... submitting an updated application accompanied by newly approved payment legislation; a decision... is expected during 2026'. I opened the page in a browser (it was previously blocked): it is dated 31/10/2024, the pre-application was submitted 14/10/2024, the legislation was NOT yet approved but pending in the Assembly, and the decision was anticipated 'next year' meaning 2025. Nothing in it supports a 2026 decision or an 'updated application'. Signal rewritten to the sourced facts; recovered the EUR 55m/year savings figure. The Nov-2026 TIPS Clone element is unaffected and remains primary-sourced from Banca d'Italia.; Bulgaria — National Assembly / DANS FIU / MONEYVAL — CONTRADICTED ON THE UPGRADED RECOMMENDATIONS. The researcher listed ten specific Recommendations (2, 12, 14, 18, 22, 25, 26, 27, 32, 33) as upgraded from Partially Compliant. The Council of Europe's own MONEYVAL statement says EIGHT Recommendations improved since the June-2025 monitoring report, in the areas of confiscation and provisional measures, the TF offence, PF-related targeted financial sanctions, NPOs, correspondent banking, sanctions and mutual legal assistance — which does not match the researcher's numbered list. The fabricated-looking list has been REMOVED and replaced with the CoE's own subject-matter description. ADDED the precise ratings split the researcher lacked: 13 compliant, 27 largely compliant, all 40 at C or LC. Source upgraded from BTA (press) to the Council of Europe MONEYVAL page (regulator). ALSO CORRECTED: the researcher's 'FATF decision on grey-list removal is expected by END-2026' — no such timetable appears in any official source, and the FATF grey-list text I read this run gives no on-site or decision date. That expectation has been removed from the signal and the likelihood field per hard rule 1; a wrong deadline is worse than no deadline. The unverified Darjaven vestnik numbers are retained but explicitly marked do-not-quote.; European Commission / all EU obliged entities — THE MOST SIGNIFICANT ERROR IN THE SET. The researcher merged the two delegated acts and attributed the Bolivia/BVI/six-removals change to both. I re-read the full text of 2026/46 on EUR-Lex (via Chrome, as the ELI page returns an empty body to automated fetch): it is titled 'amending Delegated Regulation (EU) 2016/1675 to add Russia to the list of high-risk third countries with strategic deficiencies', adopted 03/12/2025, and inserts an entirely new point IV in the Annex whose sole entry is the Russian Federation. Only 2026/83 (of 04/12/2025) covers Bolivia, the British Virgin Islands and the six removals. Confirmed unchanged: OJ publication 09/01/2026, entry into force 29/01/2026 (twentieth day after publication), and the 41-to-35 net count for the FATF-derived categories. Signal text rewritten to carry both acts correctly. opp_size raised S→M because the Russia listing is a materially larger remediation than a routine list refresh, and because the new Annex category is a structural change to country-risk tables rather than a data update.; Banka Slovenije — CONTRADICTED IN PART. The ZDPSI scope (ATMs, payment terminals, e-banking) and the uneven-readiness quote are fine as press-sourced context. But the load-bearing claim — that Banka Slovenije formally notified PSPs and called on them to present planned accessibility approaches — has no primary support. Searching bsi.si returned no such communication. The one Banka Slovenije primary document on ZDPSI, the National Payments Council 35th-session minutes of 21/03/2025, records the opposite posture: DS1 concluded ZDPSI itself would address vulnerable-group user experience and that no further working-group activity in the area is foreseen. Confidence cut Medium -> Low, likelihood cut High -> Low-Medium, source re-pointed from the RTV press piece to the Banka Slovenije minutes, and the 'central bank has already asked for plans' budget argument struck from implies. Row kept because the underlying statutory obligation is real.; National Bank of Ukraine (NBU) — AML enforcement — THE FINE ALLOCATION WAS WRONG. The researcher assigned UAH 4,096,000 to Universal Bank and UAH 100,000 to AI Lombard. Interfax-Ukraine's 07/07/2026 report of the NBU release states the opposite: AI Lombard received UAH 4,096,000 for financial-monitoring breaches PLUS a separate UAH 100,000 for FX violations, while UNIVERSAL BANK RECEIVED A WRITTEN WARNING ONLY AND NO FINE. Corrected in the signal text. Quoting a UAH 4.1m fine against a named bank that was not fined would be a serious credibility failure in a sales conversation. Confirmed unchanged: PUMB UAH 10,000,000 + written warning, Ukrsibbank UAH 400,000, Tascombank UAH 200,000, and the 'four banks and one non-bank' framing. Source tier DOWNGRADED from regulator to press and confidence to Low because bank.gov.ua returned 403 and the figures now rest on a news agency's reading of the release. The researcher's added detail about aggressor-state UBOs and late threshold reporting is not corroborated in the Interfax account and has been removed.; Slovak Republic — National Council / NBS-supervised obliged entities / Financial Intelligence Unit (FSJ) — CONTRADICTED ON THE PENALTY CEILING. The researcher reported 'up to EUR 1,000,000 generally and up to EUR 5,000,000 or 10% of annual turnover for banks and financial institutions', flagging it as unchecked. Multiple independent Slovak professional sources (SKDP, Podnikajte.sk, Semančín) state the ceiling as up to EUR 1,000,000, and NO source found this run supports a EUR 5,000,000 or 10%-of-turnover bank ceiling. The EUR 5m figure has been REMOVED and an explicit do-not-quote warning inserted — hard rule 1. CONFIRMED: amendment of Act 297/2008, effect 01/06/2026, the goAML registration duty with the 30/11/2026 deadline and 30-day rule for new entities, and the prohibition on relying solely on the RPO. ADDED, and commercially useful: the independent-verification duty applies to EXISTING relationships during ongoing monitoring, not just new clients — that materially enlarges the remediation scope; and restricted live access to the public BO register from 10/07/2026. REMOVED as uncorroborated: the 14/04/2026 adoption date and the trade-licence character requirement with a 30/09/2026 compliance date. Tier downgraded primary→press since slov-lex yields only the title to automated fetch and every substantive figure is law/tax-firm sourced.; PSR Articles 85a and 108a — provisions applying from day one — Article 112's day-one carve-out for Arts 85a and 108a is CONFIRMED verbatim. But two substantive descriptions were wrong. (1) Art 85a: researcher wrote 'recurring credit transfers initiated by the payer'; the actual text reads 'in the context of one or several recurring credit transfers initiated by the payer's payment service provider ... (a) the credit transfers are initiated by the payment service provider of the payer following a request from the payee' — a payee-triggered, PSP-initiated flow with three cumulative conditions the researcher did not mention. Corrected in the signal. (2) Art 108a: researcher described it vaguely as governing 'certain payment transactions'; the text shows it is specifically a derogation excluding electronic-money-token and crypto-asset-service-provider transactions from PSD2 until 21 months after entry into force. Corrected. Both corrections narrow the commercial claim, so opp_size held at S.; Digital euro services by PSPs in non-euro-area Member States — Regulation 2023/0211(COD) / COM(2023) 368 — CITATION AND DATE BOTH WRONG, substance largely right. I re-fetched the OEIL URL the researcher supplied. It returns a document summary dated 28/06/2023 for procedure 2023/0211(COD) — NOT 2023/0212(COD) as the row's entity field claimed, and NOT dated 09/07/2026 as the source_date claimed. The researcher had effectively dated a 2023 Commission proposal summary to a 2026 plenary event. This matters practically, not just pedantically: the non-euro-area arrangements live in their OWN regulation (COM(2023) 368), which can move on a different timetable from the main digital euro file, so an account team tracking only 2023/0212(COD) would miss developments affecting RO, CZ and HU. Entity, procedure reference and source_date all corrected, and I added the tracking consequence to 'implies'. ALSO UNVERIFIED: the 'prior EU agreement and/or arrangements concluded between the ECB and the national central bank' condition is not in this summary — it may sit in the main proposal's recitals, but it is not evidenced here, and the Montenegro/Kosovo row leans on the same unverified mechanism. Confidence cut from Medium to Low. Kept because the core substance — non-euro PSPs may distribute under home-state supervision, no mandatory acceptance in RO/CZ/HU — is confirmed and the negative finding is useful.
- **Unconfirmed (single/weak source, kept at Low) (19):** Austria - ID Austria / eAusweise, Deputy Ministry of Social Welfare (Cyprus), Association of Cyprus Banks / Cypriot banks, Central Bank of Cyprus (CBC), Česká národní banka (ČNB), Republic of Serbia - eID.gov.rs portal / ConsentID and cloud qualified electronic signature, Ministarstvo pravosuda, uprave i digitalne transformacije - Croatia, NBRNM (North Macedonia), FBA/ABRS (Bosnia & Herzegovina), BQK (Kosovo) — no DORA mirror yet, Government of North Macedonia / Financial Intelligence Unit (Управа за финансиско разузнавање, UFR) / NBRNM-supervised banks, IDDEEA (Agency for Identification Documents, Registers and Data Exchange) - Bosnia & Herzegovina, Republic of Albania - Kuvendi / AKSHI / Albanian banks, Ministrstvo za digitalno preobrazbo (Ministry of Digital Transformation) - Slovenia, Deputy Ministry of Research, Innovation and Digital Policy - Cyprus, Ministarstvo javne uprave (Ministry of Public Administration) - Montenegro, Bankart / Slovenian banks (Flik), AMF / Bank of Albania / Ministry of Finance, National EUDI wallet programmes across the footprint, CBCG / Ministry of Finance (ME), NBRNM (MK), Republika Srpska / Brcko / FBiH (BA), Footprint national competent authorities — PSD3/PSR transposition status

**Operator requests — gated / paywalled / blocked sources to fetch or verify manually:**
- **DORA (digital operational resilience):** 1) Sluzbeni list Crne Gore br. 14/26 - full text of the Montenegrin Zakon o digitalnoj operativnoj otpornosti finansijskog sektora (gazette not freely indexed) to fix the publication date and confirm whether any provisions bite before EU accession. 2) Sluzben vesnik na RSM br. 78/18 - NBRNM Decision on the Methodology for bank information-system security (nbrm.mk Cloudflare-blocked). 3) Darjaven vestnik br. 17 of 13/02/2026 - Bulgarian Law amending the Cybersecurity Act (NIS2), to replace the press citation with the primary text. 4) Fletorja Zyrtare (Albania) publication of the Bank of Albania Regulation on Digital Operational Resilience adopted 01/07/2026, to fix its entry-into-force date. 5) The SZTFH rendelet on the cybersecurity-audit procedure and capped fee, as published in Magyar Kozlony, to confirm the statutory periodicity of the Hungarian audit cycle.
- **Instant payments & SEPA (incl. SCT Inst, VoP):** 1) Open the two blocked BQK pages in a browser and capture their dates plus the name of the newly approved Kosovo payment regulation: https://bqk-kos.org/news/cbk-delegation-meets-with-directorate-general-for-financial-stability-financial-services-and-capital-markets-union-of-the-european-commission-dg-fisma-pre-application-for-kosovos-sepa-mem/?lang=en and https://bqk-kos.org/news/mbahet-punetoria-mbi-pagesat-e-shpejta-dhe-projektin-e-tips-clone-per-te-diskutuar-aspektet-teknike-dhe-operacionale-per-zbatim-te-suksesshem/?lang=en . 2) Retrieve the full text of NBU Board Resolution No.23 of 04/03/2026 from bank.gov.ua or zakon.rada.gov.ua. 3) Retrieve the CBBH primary press release and instant-payment scheme rulebook from cbbh.ba for the BiH participation criteria and current live-bank list. 4) Retrieve the Commission Implementing Regulation adopting EBA/ITS/2025/02 from EUR-Lex to fix the first IPR reporting reference period.
- **PSD3 / PSR:** none - both Council compromise texts (ST-8221-2026-INIT and ST-8222-2026-INIT) were retrieved and fully extracted locally; the previous run's operator request for these PDFs is discharged. Nothing on this workstream is currently gated or paywalled.
- **ISO 20022 migration:** 1) SWIFT "CBPR+ roadmap beyond November 2025" PDF and the current SR2026 structured-address FAQ from swift.com — blocked to automated fetch for two consecutive runs; an operator with a MySwift login should download both. This is the single most important outstanding document in this workstream. 2) National Bank of North Macedonia (nbrm.mk) — the MIPS payment-system pages and the "Регулатива за платните системи" section, blocked by Cloudflare. Needed: whether MIPS has any published ISO 20022 or structured-address timeline, and the current SEPA-scheme adherence status of Macedonian banks. 3) MNB (Hungary) — the VIBER "MX szabványváltás" annex set and VIBER Üzleti feltételek, plus a direct answer on whether hybrid postal addresses will be enabled in VIBER before 14/11/2026. The mnb.hu PDF endpoint did not serve a valid PDF to curl. 4) TRANSFOND / BNR (Romania) — the ReGIS 2026 release notes and the "Reglementări SENT" documentation, to fix the exact ReGIS 2026 go-live weekend and establish whether the SENT retail ACH migrates at the same time. Participant-only material is likely; Printec's Romanian team (BCR relationship) is the better route. 5) NBP / KIR (Poland) — the SORBNET3 participant rulebook and the KIR ELIXIR ISO 20022 migration schedule; nbp.pl is behind an Imperva bot wall. Needed to reconcile the CPMI "June 2025" SORBNET3 go-live against the baseline's 08/09/2025 and to verify the H1-2027 ELIXIR date, currently bank-marketing-tier only. 6) CBBH (Bosnia & Herzegovina) — confirmation of which EPC rulebook version is currently DESIGNATED as current for domestic credit payments in Žiro kliring, and the consolidated operating rules including the amendment in Službeni glasnik BiH br. 09/26 of 06/02/2026. That designation decides whether the 15/11/2026 structured-address ban becomes a domestic BiH obligation. 7) NBS (Serbia) — the RTGS/Kliring technical documentation referenced by Uputstvo G. br. 3673 of 13/05/2026, to find any MT retirement date; carried over from the checkpointed session and still open.
- **Digital euro & CBDC:** 1) Bloomberg, 04/05/2026, 'Montenegro Says EU Working to Fix Euro Issue Hindering Accession' (https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2026-05-04/montenegro-says-eu-working-to-fix-euro-issue-hindering-accession) — paywalled; needed to confirm whether the EU proposal on Montenegrin unilateral euro use was tabled by end-May 2026 and whether it touches digital euro access. 2) Council of the EU general approach texts ST-16695-2025-INIT and ST-16696-2025-INIT (17/12/2025) — data.consilium.europa.eu returned HTTP 500; needed for the Council's exact wording on holding limits, merchant fee caps and the online/offline payer choice. 3) EUR-Lex CELEX:52023PC0369 full text — bot-blocked; needed to quote Article 13(3)(b) on ATM/cash funding and defunding verbatim. 4) Piraeus Bank and National Bank of Greece official press releases of ~20/07/2026 on digital euro pilot participation, to upgrade the Greek row from press to primary.

IO.3 supervision, IO.4 preventive measures, IO.5 legal persons/UBO) and the Recommended Actions addressed to the financial sector. 2) https://www.coe.int/en/web/moneyval/-/outcome-of-moneyval-71st-plenary-meeting — MONEYVAL 71st Plenary (26-29/05/2026) outcomes (Cloudflare 403). 3) https://www.fatf-gafi.org/en/publications/High-risk-and-other-monitored-jurisdictions/increased-monitoring-june-2026.html — FATF June-2026 increased-monitoring statement (Cloudflare 403). Needed: the statutory reference for the OeNB-to-FMA sanctions transfer and any 2026 FMA Rundschreiben on merged AML/sanctions inspection. 5) Darjaven vestnik (dv.parliament.bg) SG No. 16/10.02.2026 and SG No. 51/05.06.2026 — the 2026 ZMIP amending acts (lex.bg returns a SOAP "Unauthorized" error; strategy.bg renders a login page). The gazette numbers are currently secondary-sourced and must not be quoted to a customer until verified. 6) EUR-Lex national transposition measures (NIM) list for CELEX 32024L1640 — returns an empty body to automated fetch; needed to confirm per-country AMLD6 transposition status for AT/BG/HR/CY/CZ/GR/HU/RO/SK/SI. 7) Zbierka zakonov text of Slovak Act 73/2026 Z. z. — to confirm the 14/04/2026 adoption, 01/06/2026 and 10/07/2026 effective dates, the 30/11/2026 goAML registration deadline and the EUR 5m / 10%-turnover bank penalty ceiling, all currently sourced from a KPMG/danovky.sk summary. NOTE (Rule 9): the AMLD6 beneficial-ownership access questions and any AMLR interpretation points raised with customers involve contested regulatory interpretation and personal-data access rights — these must go to Printec's DPO / Compliance team, not be answered from a vendor position.
- **eIDAS 2 / EUDI wallet & eID:** 1) The full text of Regulation (EU) 2024/1624 (AMLR) Article 22 — EUR-Lex serves zero-byte HTTP 202 responses for CELEX 32024R1624 on all routes; an operator should manually download the Official Journal PDF (OJ L, 2024/1624, 19/06/2024) so the EUDI-wallet-as-customer-due-diligence link can be confirmed. This is the highest-value outstanding item in the workstream. 2) Bulgarian Darjaven Vestnik issue 55/2026 — the alleged amendment to the Zakon za elektronnata identifikatsiya carried in the previous run's findings and unverifiable from three separate Bulgarian sources. 3) The Bulgarian draft EUDI wallet law PDF from the strategy.bg consultation Id=8207 (portal requires a login). 4) The Slovak NBU document 'Narodna certifikacna schema europskej penazenky digitalnej identity obcana – EUDIW-SK' (effective 22/06/2026) from nbu.gov.sk, to confirm whether bank-side relying-party components fall inside the certification scope. 5) The Montenegro–Kosovo arrangement on mutual recognition of electronic identity and electronic signature (aktuelno.me returns 403). 6) The exact publication date of Sluzhben vesnik na RSM issue 135/25, needed to fix the entry-into-force day and therefore the 12-month alignment deadline for existing North Macedonian eID scheme issuers.
- **Euro adoption (Bulgaria + pipeline):** 1) Bloomberg, 'Hungary to Meet Euro Criteria by 2030, New Finance Minister Says', 12/05/2026 - paywalled; the exact wording of Finance Minister Andras Karman's commitment and any date detail would let the Hungary row be upgraded from Low. 2) BNB Issue Department implementing instructions ('ukazaniya') under Ordinances No.18, No.46 and No.47 - the electronic reporting formats, the Annex IV submission specification and the machine control-test procedure; these appear to be circulated to supervised banks rather than published, so an operator with a Bulgarian bank contact should request them. 3) European Commission Convergence Report 2026 full PDF (Institutional Paper 343, 176pp) - the landing page was read but the per-country legal-convergence annexes were not; useful if a detailed CZ/RO legal-alignment gap list is wanted. 4) presidency.ro press release of 01/08/2026 on the euro political agreement - not located through open search; a direct check of the Romanian Presidency press archive would give the primary.
- **MiCA / crypto-asset rules:** 1) European Commission '2026-mica-review-targeted-consultation-document_en.pdf' (https://finance.ec.europa.eu/document/download/62be7015-f066-4fac-b74e-71bacdbcc9f5_en) - large consultation PDF, worth a full read for the ESMA-centralisation and stablecoin questions before Printec or its bank customers respond by 30/09/2026. 2) FMA Austria news item 'Ende der MiCAR-Uebergangsfrist' (https://www.fma.gv.at/ende-der-micar-uebergangsfrist-esma-legt-klare-vorgaben-fuer-nicht-zugelassene-anbieter-fest/) - Cloudflare-gated, needs a real browser session. 3) MNB primary press release / institution-register entry for the 20/07/2026 Tiwala Solutions Kft CASP licence, from mnb.hu (JS-rendered). 4) Greek FEK text of Law 5193/2025 and HCMC Decision 8/1059 of 30/07/2025 - needed before any Greek sanction figure can be quoted. 5) Monitorul Oficial text of Romanian OUG 10/2025.
- **Horizon scan — emerging regulation beyond the watchlist:** 1) Visa CEMEA and Mastercard Europe/CEMEA operating-regulation and business-enhancement bulletins for 2026 - these set the binding ATM/POS acceptance and security deadlines across all 17 markets and are behind scheme partner portals; an operator with acquirer-side access should pull the 2026 releases. 2) Senate of Romania file for legislative initiative B409/2026 (amending Legea 209/2019) - the senat.ro initiative-file page and the attached PDF, to verify the registration date, the exact obligation wording and the RON 50,000-100,000 penalty. 3) The Slovenian Ministry of Finance draft 'Zakon o dostopnosti gotovine in brezplacnih osnovnih placilnih storitvah' as published on e-uprava.gov.si/eDemokracija - the full draft PDF with articles, transition period and penalties. 4) The annex to Hungarian NGM Decree 16/2025 (V.29.) listing the settlements requiring ATMs, plus the MNB per-PSP allocation table - needed to size the >500-inhabitant tranche by bank.
